\frac{1}{2\pi{\mathrm{i}}}\int_{\partial D(\mu_{k},r_{*})}\frac{\lambda^{\kappa}e^{\lambda t}}{(\lambda^{2}-\mu_{k}^{2})^{j}}{\mathrm{d}}\lambda=p_{\kappa,k,j}(t)e^{\mu_{k}t}\;\mbox{for any}\;t\in{\mathbb{R}}.

This fact allows us to infer that the norm of the residue from (
3.13
) is of order
\mathscr{O}(t^{N_{k}-1}e^{\mu_{k}t})
.

Next, we study the properties of residues of the operator-valued functions involved in the representations of cosine family and its associated sine family from Section
2
.

Lemma 3.5
.

Assume Hypothesis (H) and let
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
and
x\in\text{\rm{dom}}(A^{n})
. Then,

(i)

{\mathrm{Res}}\Bigl(\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n-1}}R_{A}(\lambda)A^{n}x,\lambda=\mu_{k}\Bigr)={\mathrm{Res}}\Bigl(\lambda e^{\lambda t}R_{A}(\lambda)x,\lambda=\mu_{k}\Bigr)
, if
\mu_{k}\neq 0
;

(ii)

{\mathrm{Res}}\Bigl(\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n-1}}R_{A}(\lambda)A^{n}x,\lambda=0\Bigr)={\mathrm{Res}}\Bigl(\lambda e^{\lambda t}R_{A}(\lambda)x,\lambda=0\Bigr)-\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}\frac{t^{2j}}{(2j)!}A^{j}x
, if
0\in{\mathscr{M}}
;

(iii)

{\mathrm{Res}}\Bigl(\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n-1}}R_{A}(\lambda)A^{n}x,\lambda=0\Bigr)=-\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}\frac{t^{2j}}{(2j)!}A^{j}x
, if
0\in\Omega_{0}\setminus{\mathscr{M}}
.

Proof.

From Lemma
3.2
(iii) we obtain that

(3.29)

\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n-1}}R_{A}(\lambda)A^{n}x=\lambda R_{A}(\lambda)x-\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2j+1}}A^{j}x\;\mbox{for any}\;\lambda\in\Omega\setminus\bigl({\mathscr{M}}\cup\{0\}\bigr),

while an elementary computation shows that

(3.30)

{\mathrm{Res}}\Bigl(\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{\ell}},\lambda=\mu\Bigr)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}0,&\mu\neq 0,\\
\frac{t^{\ell-1}}{(\ell-1)!},&\mu=0,\end{array}\right.\;\mbox{for any}\;t\in{\mathbb{R}},\ell\in{\mathbb{N}},

thus proving the lemma. ∎

Lemma 3.6
.

Assume Hypothesis (H) and let
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
x\in{\mathbb{X}}
and
y\in\text{\rm{dom}}(A^{n-1})
. Then,

(i)

{\mathrm{Res}}\Bigl(\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n}}R_{A}(\lambda)x,\lambda=\mu\Bigr)\in\text{\rm{dom}}(A)
for any
\mu\in{\mathscr{M}}\cup\{0\}
;

(ii)

A{\mathrm{Res}}\Bigl(\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n}}R_{A}(\lambda)A^{n-1}y,\lambda=\mu_{k}\Bigr)={\mathrm{Res}}\Bigl(e^{\lambda t}R_{A}(\lambda)y,\lambda=\mu_{k}\Bigr)
, if
\mu_{k}\neq 0
;

(iii)

A{\mathrm{Res}}\Bigl(\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n}}R_{A}(\lambda)A^{n-1}y,\lambda=0\Bigr)={\mathrm{Res}}\Bigl(e^{\lambda t}R_{A}(\lambda)y,\lambda=0\Bigr)-\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}\frac{t^{2j+1}}{(2j+1)!}A^{j}y
, if
\mu_{k}=0
;

(iv)

A{\mathrm{Res}}\Bigl(\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n}}R_{A}(\lambda)A^{n-1}y,\lambda=0\Bigr)=-\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}\frac{t^{2j+1}}{(2j+1)!}A^{j}y
, if
0\in\Omega_{0}\setminus{\mathscr{M}}
.

Proof.

Assertion (
3.10
) yields

(3.31)

\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n}}R_{A}(\lambda)x\in\text{\rm{dom}}(A)\;\mbox{and}\;A\Bigl(\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n}}R_{A}(\lambda)x\Bigr)=\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n-2}}R_{A}(\lambda)x-\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n}}x

for any
\lambda\in\Omega_{0}\setminus\bigl({\mathscr{M}}\cup\{0\}\bigr)
, and so (i) follows from (
3.31
) and
[
2
, Proposition 1.1.7]
. Moreover,

(3.32)

A{\mathrm{Res}}\Bigl(\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n}}R_{A}(\lambda)x,\lambda=\mu\Bigr)={\mathrm{Res}}\Bigl(\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n-2}}R_{A}(\lambda)x,\lambda=\mu\Bigr)-{\mathrm{Res}}\Bigl(\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n}},\lambda=\mu\Bigr)x

for any
\mu\in{\mathscr{M}}\cup\{0\}
. Using Lemma
3.2
(iii),

(3.33)

\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n-2}}R_{A}(\lambda)A^{n-1}y=e^{\lambda t}R_{A}(\lambda)y-\sigma_{n}(\lambda,t,y)\;\mbox{for any}\;\lambda\in\Omega_{0}\setminus\Bigl(\{\mu_{k}:k=1,\dots,m\}\cup\{0\}\Bigr),

where the function
\sigma_{n}:{\mathbb{C}}\setminus\{0\}\times{\mathbb{R}}\times\text{\rm{dom}}(A^{n-1})\to{\mathbb{X}}
is defined by

(3.34)

\sigma_{n}(\lambda,t,y)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}0,&n=1,\\
\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{n-2}\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2j+2}}A^{j}y,&n\geq 2.\end{array}\right.

We note that

(3.35)

\sigma_{n}(\lambda,t,y)+\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n}}A^{n-1}y=\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2j+2}}A^{j}y

for any
\lambda\in\Omega_{0}\setminus\bigl({\mathscr{M}}\cup\{0\}\bigr)
,
t\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
y\in\text{\rm{dom}}(A^{n-1})
. From (
3.32
), (
3.33
), (
3.34
) and (
3.35
) we obtain that

(3.36)

A{\mathrm{Res}}\Bigl(\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n}}R_{A}(\lambda)A^{n-1}y,\lambda=\mu\Bigr)={\mathrm{Res}}\Bigl(e^{\lambda t}R_{A}(\lambda)y,\lambda=\mu\Bigr)-\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}{\mathrm{Res}}\Bigl(\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2j+2}},\lambda=\mu\Bigr)A^{j}y

for any
\mu\in{\mathscr{M}}\cup\{0\}
. Assertions (ii)-(iv) follow from (
3.30
) and (
3.36
). ∎

In the sequel we need an estimate of
\|AR_{A}(\lambda)_{|{\mathbb{W}}}\|_{\mathscr{B}({\mathbb{W}},{\mathbb{X}})}
for
\lambda\in\Omega_{0}\setminus{\mathbb{C}}_{\omega}^{+}
analogous to (
2.28
) . We recall the definition of the space
{\mathbb{W}}
in (
2.4
).

Lemma 3.7
.

Assume Hypothesis (H) and let
M_{1}
is the constant defined in Lemma
2.3
. Then,

(3.37)

\|AR_{A}(\lambda)w\|\leq M_{1}\Bigl(\omega^{-1}+4\bigl(|\lambda|+\omega\bigr)h_{1}(|\lambda|)h_{2}({\mathrm{Re}}\lambda)\Bigr)\|w\|_{\mathbb{W}}

for any
w\in{\mathbb{W}}
,
\lambda\in\Omega_{0}\setminus{\mathbb{C}}_{\omega}^{+}
such that
|{\mathrm{Im}}\lambda|>\max\limits_{1\leq k\leq m}|{\mathrm{Im}}\mu_{k}|
.

Proof.

Fix
w\in{\mathbb{W}}
and
\lambda\in\Omega_{0}\setminus{\mathbb{C}}_{\omega}^{+}
such that
|{\mathrm{Im}}\lambda|>\max\limits_{1\leq k\leq m}|{\mathrm{Im}}\mu_{k}|
and set
\mu=2\omega+|{\mathrm{Re}}\lambda|+{\mathrm{i}}{\mathrm{Im}}\lambda
. One can readily check that

(3.38)

{\mathrm{Re}}\mu\geq 2\omega,\;|\mu|\leq 2\omega+|\lambda|\;\mbox{and}\;\frac{{\mathrm{Re}}\mu-{\mathrm{Re}}\lambda}{{\mathrm{Re}}\mu-\omega}=\frac{2\omega+|{\mathrm{Re}}\lambda|-{\mathrm{Re}}\lambda}{\omega+|{\mathrm{Re}}\lambda|}\leq 2.

Since
{\mathrm{Im}}\mu={\mathrm{Im}}\lambda
, by Lemma
3.2
(ii) and the resolvent identity we obtain

(3.39)

AR_{A}(\lambda)w=AR(\mu^{2},A)w+({\mathrm{Re}}\mu-{\mathrm{Re}}\lambda)(\lambda+\mu)R_{A}(\lambda)AR(\mu^{2},A)w.

From Hypothesis (H), (
2.28
), (
3.38
) and (
3.39
) we conclude that

\displaystyle\|AR_{A}(\lambda)w\|

\displaystyle\leq\|AR(\mu^{2},A)w\|+({\mathrm{Re}}\mu-{\mathrm{Re}}\lambda)|(\lambda+\mu)|\,\|R_{A}(\lambda)AR(\mu^{2},A)w\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{M_{1}}{{\mathrm{Re}}\mu-\omega}\|w\|_{\mathbb{W}}+M_{1}\bigl(|\lambda|+|\mu|\bigr)\frac{{\mathrm{Re}}\mu-{\mathrm{Re}}\lambda}{{\mathrm{Re}}\mu-\omega}h_{1}(|\lambda|)h_{2}({\mathrm{Re}}\lambda)\|w\|_{\mathbb{W}}

(3.40)

\displaystyle\leq M_{1}\Bigl(\omega^{-1}+4\bigl(|\lambda|+\omega\bigr)h_{1}(|\lambda|)h_{2}({\mathrm{Re}}\lambda)\Bigr)\|w\|_{\mathbb{W}},

proving the lemma.
∎

We are now ready to see what are the conditions needed to switch integration in the representation formulas for the cosine family and its associated sine family given in
Lemma
2.10
and Lemma
2.11
to the path along the complex curve
{\mathrm{Re}}\lambda=g_{0}({\mathrm{Im}}\lambda)+\varepsilon
for some
\varepsilon>0
small enough. The idea is to integrate along a curve parallel to the left boundary of the set
\Omega_{0}
, such that all the singularities of the operator-valued function
R_{A}(\cdot)
are located to the right of the curve cf. Figure 2. Since
{\mathrm{Re}}\mu_{k}>g_{0}({\mathrm{Im}}\mu_{k})
for any
k=1,\dots,m
by Hypothesis H(ii), we observe that

(3.41)

\varepsilon_{0}:=\frac{1}{4}\min\bigl\{\omega-\sup_{s\in{\mathbb{R}}}g_{0}(s),{\mathrm{Re}}\mu_{1}-g_{0}({\mathrm{Im}}\mu_{1}),\dots,{\mathrm{Re}}\mu_{m}-g_{0}({\mathrm{Im}}\mu_{m})\bigr\}>0.

For any
a>\max_{1\leq k\leq m}|{\mathrm{Im}}\mu_{k}|
and
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0})
we consider the closed simple path
\Gamma_{a}^{\varepsilon}
defined as the union
\Gamma_{a,\gamma}\cup\Gamma_{a,{\mathrm{up}}}^{\varepsilon}\cup\Gamma_{a,{\mathrm{down}}}^{\varepsilon}\cup-\Gamma_{a,{\mathrm{mid}}}^{\varepsilon}
and oriented counterclockwise, where

\displaystyle\Gamma_{a,\gamma}:=\overline{\gamma-{\mathrm{i}}a,\gamma+{\mathrm{i}}a},\;\Gamma_{a,{\mathrm{up}}}^{\varepsilon}:=\overline{\gamma+{\mathrm{i}}a,g_{0}(a)+\varepsilon+{\mathrm{i}}a},\;\Gamma_{a,{\mathrm{down}}}^{\varepsilon}=\overline{g_{0}(-a)+\varepsilon-{\mathrm{i}}a,\gamma-{\mathrm{i}}a},

(3.42)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{a,{\mathrm{mid}}}^{\varepsilon}:\lambda=g_{0}(s)+\varepsilon+{\mathrm{i}}s,\;s\in[-a,a].

Figure 2. The path
\Gamma_{a}^{\varepsilon}
superimposed over the set
\Omega_{0}
.

Remark 3.8
.

We recall an elementary analysis result needed in the sequel. Let
G_{1}:[0,\infty)\to{\mathbb{R}}
be a measurable function such that
\lim\limits_{s\to\infty}G_{1}(s)=0
and
G_{2}\in L^{1}({\mathbb{R}})
. Then, by Lebesgue’s Dominated Convergence Theorem,

(3.43)

\lim_{a\to\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}}G_{1}(\sqrt{\xi^{2}+a^{2}})G_{2}(\xi){\mathrm{d}}\xi=0.

Lemma 3.9
.

Assume Hypothesis (H) and, in addition that
b:=\inf\limits_{s\in{\mathbb{R}}}g_{0}(s)>-\infty
. Then,

(3.44)

\lim_{a\to\infty}\int_{\Gamma_{a,{\mathrm{up}}}^{\varepsilon}}\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{k}}R_{A}(\lambda){\mathrm{d}}\lambda=\lim_{a\to\infty}\int_{\Gamma_{a,{\mathrm{down}}}^{\varepsilon}}\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{k}}R_{A}(\lambda){\mathrm{d}}\lambda=0\;\mbox{for any}\;t\geq 0,\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}),

in the
\mathscr{B}({\mathbb{X}})
-norm for any
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
with
k\geq 2n_{0}-1
, where
n_{0}
is introduced in (
3.4
).

Proof.

Fix
t\geq 0
,
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0})
and
a>\max_{1\leq k\leq m}|{\mathrm{Im}}\mu_{k}|
. From (
3
) we have

\displaystyle\int_{\Gamma_{a,{\mathrm{up}}}^{\varepsilon}}\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{k}}R_{A}(\lambda){\mathrm{d}}\lambda

\displaystyle=-\int_{g_{0}(a)+\varepsilon}^{\gamma}\frac{e^{(\xi+{\mathrm{i}}a)t}}{(\xi+{\mathrm{i}}a)^{k}}R_{A}(\xi+{\mathrm{i}}a){\mathrm{d}}\xi,

(3.45)

\displaystyle\int_{\Gamma_{a,{\mathrm{down}}}^{\varepsilon}}\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{k}}R_{A}(\lambda){\mathrm{d}}\lambda

\displaystyle=\int_{g_{0}(-a)+\varepsilon}^{\gamma}\frac{e^{(\xi-{\mathrm{i}}a)t}}{(\xi-{\mathrm{i}}a)^{k}}R_{A}(\xi-{\mathrm{i}}a){\mathrm{d}}\xi.

From Hypothesis (H)(iii) we obtain

(3.46)

\Bigl\|\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{k}}R_{A}(\lambda)\Bigr\|=\frac{e^{({\mathrm{Re}}\lambda)t}}{|\lambda|^{k}}\|R_{A}(\lambda)\|\leq\frac{e^{\omega t}}{|\lambda|^{k}}h_{1}(|\lambda|)h_{2}({\mathrm{Re}}\lambda)\leq e^{\omega t}\bigl(\sup_{b\leq s\leq\gamma}h_{2}(s)\bigr)\frac{h_{1}(|\lambda|)}{|\lambda|^{k}},

whenever
\lambda\in\mathrm{Range}(\Gamma_{a,{\mathrm{up}}}^{\varepsilon})\cup\mathrm{Range}(\Gamma_{a,{\mathrm{down}}}^{\varepsilon})
and
{\mathrm{Re}}\lambda\leq\omega
. It follows that

(3.47)

\Bigg\|\int_{g_{0}(\pm a)+\varepsilon}^{\omega}\frac{e^{(\xi\pm{\mathrm{i}}a)t}}{(\xi\pm{\mathrm{i}}a)^{k}}R_{A}(\xi\pm{\mathrm{i}}a){\mathrm{d}}\xi\Bigg\|\leq e^{\omega t}\bigl(\sup_{b\leq s\leq\gamma}h_{2}(s)\bigr)\int_{b}^{\omega}\frac{h_{1}(\sqrt{\xi^{2}+a^{2}})}{(\xi^{2}+a^{2})^{\frac{k}{2}}}{\mathrm{d}}\xi.

Since
\lim\limits_{s\to\infty}\frac{h_{1}(s)}{s^{k}}=0
by the assumption and
\chi_{[b,\omega]}\in L^{1}({\mathbb{R}})
, from Remark
3.8
we infer

(3.48)

\lim_{a\to\infty}\int_{b}^{\omega}\frac{h_{1}(\sqrt{\xi^{2}+a^{2}})}{(\xi^{2}+a^{2})^{\frac{k}{2}}}{\mathrm{d}}\xi=0.

From (
3.47
) and (
3.48
) we conclude that

(3.49)

\lim_{a\to\infty}\int_{g_{0}(a)+\varepsilon}^{\omega}\frac{e^{(\xi+{\mathrm{i}}a)t}}{(\xi+{\mathrm{i}}a)^{k}}R_{A}(\xi+{\mathrm{i}}a){\mathrm{d}}\xi=\lim_{a\to\infty}\int_{g_{0}(-a)+\varepsilon}^{\omega}\frac{e^{(\xi-{\mathrm{i}}a)t}}{(\xi-{\mathrm{i}}a)^{k}}R_{A}(\xi-{\mathrm{i}}a){\mathrm{d}}\xi=0.

Since
a>\max_{1\leq k\leq m}|{\mathrm{Im}}\mu_{k}|
from Lemma
3.2
(i) we have
(\omega\pm{\mathrm{i}}a)^{2}\in\rho(A)
. Moreover, from Hypothesis (H)(iii), (
2.22
) and the resolvent equation we estimate:

\displaystyle\|R_{A}(\xi\pm{\mathrm{i}}a)\|

\displaystyle\leq\|R_{A}(\omega\pm{\mathrm{i}}a)\|+\bigl\|(\xi-\omega)(\xi+\omega\pm 2{\mathrm{i}}a)R_{A}(\omega\pm{\mathrm{i}}a)R_{A}(\xi\pm{\mathrm{i}}a)\bigr\|

\displaystyle\leq\|R_{A}(\omega\pm{\mathrm{i}}a)\|+|\xi+\omega+2{\mathrm{i}}a|\,\|R_{A}(\omega\pm{\mathrm{i}}a)\|\,\|(\xi-\omega)R_{A}(\xi\pm{\mathrm{i}}a)\|

\displaystyle\leq\Bigg(1+M\sqrt{\frac{(\xi+\omega)^{2}+4a^{2}}{\xi^{2}+a^{2}}}\Bigg)h_{1}(\sqrt{\omega^{2}+a^{2}})h_{2}(\omega)

(3.50)

\displaystyle\leq\Bigg(1+M\sqrt{\frac{(\gamma+\omega)^{2}+4a^{2}}{\omega^{2}+a^{2}}}\Bigg)h_{1}(\sqrt{\omega^{2}+a^{2}})h_{2}(\omega)

for any
\xi\in[\omega,\gamma]
. From (
3
) it follows that

(3.51)

\Bigg\|\int_{\omega}^{\gamma}\frac{e^{(\xi\pm{\mathrm{i}}a)t}}{(\xi\pm{\mathrm{i}}a)^{k}}R_{A}(\xi\pm{\mathrm{i}}a){\mathrm{d}}\xi\Bigg\|\leq(\gamma-\omega)h_{2}(\omega)e^{\gamma t}\Bigg(1+M\sqrt{\frac{(\gamma+\omega)^{2}+4a^{2}}{\omega^{2}+a^{2}}}\Bigg)\frac{h_{1}(\sqrt{\omega^{2}+a^{2}})}{(\omega^{2}+a^{2})^{\frac{k}{2}}}.

Since
\lim\limits_{a\to\infty}\frac{h_{1}(\sqrt{\omega^{2}+a^{2}})}{(\omega^{2}+a^{2})^{\frac{k}{2}}}=0
from (
3.51
) we obtain that

(3.52)

\lim_{a\to\infty}\int_{\omega}^{\gamma}\frac{e^{(\xi+{\mathrm{i}}a)t}}{(\xi+{\mathrm{i}}a)^{k}}R_{A}(\xi+{\mathrm{i}}a){\mathrm{d}}\xi=\lim_{a\to\infty}\int_{\omega}^{\gamma}\frac{e^{(\xi-{\mathrm{i}}a)t}}{(\xi-{\mathrm{i}}a)^{k}}R_{A}(\xi-{\mathrm{i}}a){\mathrm{d}}\xi=0.

The lemma follows shortly from (
3
), (
3.49
) and (
3.52
). ∎

Remark 3.10
.

From (
3.41
) and Hypothesis (H) one can readily check that
{\mathscr{M}}
is contained in the interior of the set enclosed by the path
\Gamma_{a}^{\varepsilon}
for any
a>\max\limits_{1\leq k\leq m}|{\mathrm{Im}}\mu_{k}|
,
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0})
. Similarly,
0\notin\mathrm{Range}(\Gamma_{a,{\mathrm{mid}}}^{\varepsilon})
. Moreover,
0
belongs to the interior of the set enclosed by
\Gamma_{a}^{\varepsilon}
if and only if
g_{0}(0)<0
.

Next, we recall the definition of convergence in the
principal value sense
. Let
h:\Omega\to{\mathbb{X}}
be a continuous function and
\Psi:{\mathbb{R}}\to{\mathbb{C}}
a piecewise smooth path. We say that
\displaystyle\int_{\Psi}h(\lambda){\mathrm{d}}\lambda
is
convergent in the principal value sense
if
\lim\limits_{a\to\infty}\displaystyle\int_{\Psi_{a}}h(\lambda){\mathrm{d}}\lambda
exists in
{\mathbb{X}}
norm, where
\Psi_{a}=\Psi_{|[-a,a]}
. In this case we use the notation
{\mathit{p.v.}}\displaystyle\int_{\Psi}h(\lambda){\mathrm{d}}\lambda=\lim\limits_{a\to\infty}\displaystyle\int_{\Psi_{a}}h(\lambda){\mathrm{d}}\lambda
.

We are ready to formulate the main results of this section. We introduce the path

(3.53)

\Lambda_{0}^{\varepsilon}:\lambda=g_{0}(s)+\varepsilon+{\mathrm{i}}s,\;s\in{\mathbb{R}}.

Theorem 3.11
.

Assume Hypothesis (H) and, in addition, that
b:=\inf\limits_{s\in{\mathbb{R}}}g_{0}(s)>-\infty
. Then,

(i)

The integral
\displaystyle\int_{\Lambda_{0}^{\varepsilon}}\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n-1}}R_{A}(\lambda)A^{n}x{\mathrm{d}}\lambda
is convergent in the principal value sense in
{\mathbb{X}}
for any
t\geq 0
,
x\in\text{\rm{dom}}(A^{n})
,
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0})
,
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
, with
n\geq n_{0}
;

(ii)

C(t)x=\displaystyle\sum\limits_{k=1}^{m}\sum_{j=1}^{N_{k}}p_{1,k,j}(t)e^{\mu_{k}t}(A-\mu_{k}^{2}I_{\mathbb{X}})^{j-1}P_{k}x+\frac{1}{2\pi{\mathrm{i}}}{\mathit{p.v.}}\displaystyle\int_{\Lambda_{0}^{\varepsilon}}\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n-1}}R_{A}(\lambda)A^{n}x{\mathrm{d}}\lambda+

+\chi_{[0,\infty)}(g_{0}(0))\displaystyle\sum\limits_{j=0}^{n-1}\frac{t^{2j}}{(2j)!}A^{j}x
for any
t\geq 0
,
x\in\text{\rm{dom}}(A^{n})
,
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0})
,
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
, with
n\geq n_{0}
.

The positive integer
n_{0}
is introduced in Hypothesis (H)(iv),the projection
P_{k}
and
N_{k}\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
k=1,\dots,m
, were introduced in Lemma
3.3
, and
p_{1,k,j}
is defined in (
3.28
).

Proof.

Fix
t\geq 0
,
x\in\text{\rm{dom}}(A^{n})
,
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0})
. By Remark
3.10
, (
3.41
) and (
3
), the singularities of the meromorphic function
\lambda\to\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n-1}}R_{A}(\lambda)A^{n}x:\Omega_{0}\setminus\bigl({\mathscr{M}}\cup\{0\}\bigr)\to{\mathbb{X}}
located in the interior of the set enclosed by the closed path
\Gamma_{a}^{\varepsilon}
are the finitely meromorphic points
\{\mu_{k}:k=1,\dots,m\}
and, possibly
0
, provided that
g_{0}(0)<0
. From Lemma
3.3
, Remark
3.4
, Lemma
3.5
and the Residues Theorem we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi{\mathrm{i}}}\int_{\Gamma_{a}^{\varepsilon}}\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n-1}}

\displaystyle R_{A}(\lambda)A^{n}x{\mathrm{d}}\lambda=\sum_{k=1}^{m}{\mathrm{Res}}(\lambda e^{\lambda t}R_{A}(\lambda)x,\lambda=\mu_{k})+\chi_{(-\infty,0)}(g_{0}(0))\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}\frac{t^{2j}}{(2j)!}A^{j}x

(3.54)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{m}\sum_{j=1}^{N_{k}}p_{1,k,j}(t)e^{\mu_{k}t}(A-\mu_{k}^{2}I_{\mathbb{X}})^{j-1}P_{k}x+\chi_{(-\infty,0)}(g_{0}(0))\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}\frac{t^{2j}}{(2j)!}A^{j}x.

for any
a>\max\limits_{1\leq k\leq m}|{\mathrm{Im}}\mu_{k}|+1
. The theorem now follows from Lemma
2.10
and Lemma
3.9
by passing to the limit as
a\to\infty
in (
3
). ∎

Theorem 3.12
.

Assume Hypothesis (H) and, in addition, that
b:=\inf\limits_{s\in{\mathbb{R}}}g_{0}(s)>-\infty
. Then,

(i)

The integral
\displaystyle\int_{\Lambda_{0}^{\varepsilon}}\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n}}R_{A}(\lambda)A^{n-1}w{\mathrm{d}}\lambda
belongs to
\text{\rm{dom}}(A)
and it is convergent in the principal value sense in
{\mathbb{X}}
for any
t\geq 0
,
w\in{\mathbb{W}}_{n-1}
,
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0})
,
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
, with
n\geq n_{0}
;

(ii)

S(t)w=\displaystyle\sum\limits_{k=1}^{m}\sum_{j=1}^{N_{k}}p_{0,k,j}(t)e^{\mu_{k}t}(A-\mu_{k}^{2}I_{\mathbb{X}})^{j-1}P_{k}w+\frac{1}{2\pi{\mathrm{i}}}A{\mathit{p.v.}}\displaystyle\int_{{\mathrm{Re}}\lambda=g_{0}({\mathrm{Im}}\lambda)+\varepsilon}\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n}}R_{A}(\lambda)A^{n-1}w{\mathrm{d}}\lambda

+\chi_{[0,\infty)}(g_{0}(0))\sum\limits_{j=0}^{n-1}\frac{t^{2j+1}}{(2j+1)!}A^{j}w
for any
t\geq 0
,
w\in{\mathbb{W}}_{n-1}
,
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0})
,
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
, with
n\geq n_{0}
.

We recall the definition of the projection
P_{k}
and
N_{k}\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
k=1,\dots,m
, in Lemma
3.3
, and of the polynomial
p_{1,k,j}
in (
3.28
).

Proof.

Fix
t\geq 0
,
w\in{\mathbb{W}}_{n-1}
,
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0})
. We argue as in the proof of Theorem
3.11
with (
3
) replaced by

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi{\mathrm{i}}}\int_{\Gamma_{a}^{\varepsilon}}\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n}}R_{A}(\lambda)A^{n-1}w{\mathrm{d}}\lambda

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mu\in{\mathscr{M}}\setminus\{0\}}{\mathrm{Res}}\Bigl(\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n}}R_{A}(\lambda)A^{n-1}w,\lambda=\mu\Bigr)

(3.55)

\displaystyle+\chi_{(-\infty,0)}(g_{0}(0))\,{\mathrm{Res}}\Bigl(\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n}}R_{A}(\lambda)A^{n-1}w,\lambda=0\Bigr).

for any
a>\max\limits_{1\leq k\leq m}|{\mathrm{Im}}\mu_{k}|+1
. Passing to the limit as
a\to\infty
in (
3
), from Lemma
2.11
(ii) and
Lemma
3.9
we obtain

(3.56)

\lim_{a\to\infty}\int_{\Gamma_{a,{\mathrm{mid}}}^{\varepsilon}}\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n}}R_{A}(\lambda)A^{n-1}w{\mathrm{d}}\lambda=\int_{{\mathrm{Re}}\lambda=\gamma}\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n}}R_{A}(\lambda)A^{n-1}w{\mathrm{d}}\lambda-2\pi{\mathrm{i}}{\mathscr{S}}_{n,t}w,

where the linear operator
{\mathscr{S}}_{n,t}:{\mathbb{W}}_{n-1}\to{\mathbb{X}}
is defined by

(3.57)

{\mathscr{S}}_{n,t}w=\sum_{\mu\in{\mathscr{M}}\setminus\{0\}}{\mathrm{Res}}(\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n}}R_{A}(\lambda)A^{n-1}w,\lambda=\mu)+\chi_{(-\infty,0)}(g_{0}(0))\,{\mathrm{Res}}(\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n}}R_{A}(\lambda)A^{n-1}w,\lambda=0).

From (
3.56
) we conclude that
\displaystyle\int_{\Lambda_{0}^{\varepsilon}}\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n}}R_{A}(\lambda)A^{n-1}w{\mathrm{d}}\lambda
is convergent in the principal value sense in
{\mathbb{X}}
. Moreover, since
\mathrm{Range}({\mathscr{S}}_{n,t})\subseteq\text{\rm{dom}}(A)
by Lemma
3.6
(i), from Lemma
2.11
(iii) we infer that
{\mathit{p.v.}}\displaystyle\int_{\Lambda_{0}^{\varepsilon}}\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n}}R_{A}(\lambda)A^{n-1}w{\mathrm{d}}\lambda\in\text{\rm{dom}}(A)
, proving (i).

Next, from Lemma
3.3
, Remark
3.4
and Lemma
3.6
(ii)-(iv) we obtain that
{\rm Range}({\mathscr{S}}_{n,t})\subseteq\text{\rm{dom}}(A)
and

\displaystyle A{\mathscr{S}}_{n,t}w

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{m}{\mathrm{Res}}(\lambda e^{\lambda t}R_{A}(\lambda),\lambda=\mu_{k})w+\chi_{(-\infty,0)}(g_{0}(0))\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}\frac{t^{2j}}{(2j)!}A^{j}x

(3.58)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{m}\sum_{j=1}^{N_{k}}p_{0,k,j}(t)e^{\mu_{k}t}(A-\mu_{k}^{2}I_{\mathbb{X}})^{j-1}P_{k}w+\chi_{(-\infty,0)}(g_{0}(0))\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}\frac{t^{2j+1}}{(2j+1)!}A^{j}w.

From Lemma
2.11
(iv), (
3.56
) and (
3
) it follows that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi{\mathrm{i}}}A{\mathit{p.v.}}\displaystyle\int_{\Lambda_{0}^{\varepsilon}}\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n}}R_{A}(\lambda)A^{n-1}w{\mathrm{d}}\lambda=\frac{1}{2\pi{\mathrm{i}}}A\int_{{\mathrm{Re}}\lambda=\gamma}\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n}}R_{A}(\lambda)A^{n-1}w{\mathrm{d}}\lambda-A{\mathscr{S}}_{n,t}w

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=S(t)w-\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}\frac{t^{2j+1}}{(2j+1)!}A^{j}w-\sum_{k=1}^{m}\sum_{j=1}^{N_{k}}p_{0,k,j}(t)e^{\mu_{k}t}(A-\mu_{k}^{2}I_{\mathbb{X}})^{j-1}P_{k}w

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\chi_{(-\infty,0)}(g_{0}(0))\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}\frac{t^{2j+1}}{(2j+1)!}A^{j}w

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=S(t)w-\chi_{[0,\infty)}(g_{0}(0))\sum\limits_{j=0}^{n-1}\frac{t^{2j+1}}{(2j+1)!}A^{j}w-\sum_{k=1}^{m}\sum_{j=1}^{N_{k}}p_{0,k,j}(t)e^{\mu_{k}t}(A-\mu_{k}^{2}I_{\mathbb{X}})^{j-1}P_{k}w,

proving the theorem. ∎

We note that the conclusion of Theorem
3.12
remain true if (
3.4
) is replaced by
\lim\limits_{s\to\infty}\frac{h_{1}(s)}{s^{2n_{0}}}=0
. Out next task is to estimate the integral terms in the representations of the cosine and sine families given in Theorem
3.11
and Theorem
3.12
, respectively. To obtain appropriate estimates we have to find a balance between the smoothness of the initial conditions of (
1.1
) and the decay properties at
+\infty
of the function
h_{1}
introduced in Hypothesis (H).

Lemma 3.13
.

Assume Hypothesis (H). Then, the following assertions hold true:

(i)

There exists
M_{\mathrm{mid}}^{\mathrm{c}}:{\mathbb{N}}\times(0,\infty)\times(0,\varepsilon_{0})\to(0,\infty)
such that

(3.59)

\Bigg\|\int_{\Gamma_{a,{\mathrm{mid}}}^{\varepsilon}}\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n-1}}R_{A}(\lambda)A^{n}x{\mathrm{d}}\lambda\Bigg\|\leq M_{\mathrm{mid}}^{\mathrm{c}}(n,a,\varepsilon)e^{(\sup_{s\in{\mathbb{R}}}g_{0}(s)+\varepsilon)t}\|A^{n-1}x\|

for any
t\geq 0
,
x\in\text{\rm{dom}}(A^{n})
,
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0})
,
a>0
;

(ii)

There exists
M_{\mathrm{mid}}^{\mathrm{s}}:{\mathbb{N}}\times(0,\infty)\times(0,\varepsilon_{0})\to(0,\infty)
such that

(3.60)

\Bigg\|A\int_{\Gamma_{a,{\mathrm{mid}}}^{\varepsilon}}\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n}}R_{A}(\lambda)A^{n-1}w{\mathrm{d}}\lambda\Bigg\|\leq M_{\mathrm{mid}}^{\mathrm{s}}(n,a,\varepsilon)e^{(\sup_{s\in{\mathbb{R}}}g_{0}(s)+\varepsilon)t}\|A^{n-1}w\|

for any
t\geq 0
,
x\in{\mathbb{W}}_{n-1}
,
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0})
,
a>0
.

Proof.

Fix
t\geq 0
,
x\in\text{\rm{dom}}(A^{n})
,
w\in{\mathbb{W}}_{n-1}
,
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0})
. From (
3.8
) and (
3.10
) we obtain that

\displaystyle\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n-1}}R_{A}(\lambda)A^{n}x

\displaystyle=\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n-3}}R_{A}(\lambda)A^{n-1}x-\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n-1}}A^{n-1}x,

(3.61)

\displaystyle\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n}}AR_{A}(\lambda)A^{n-1}w

\displaystyle=\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n-2}}R_{A}(\lambda)A^{n-1}w-\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n}}A^{n-1}w.

for any
\lambda\in\Omega_{0}\setminus\bigl({\mathscr{M}}\cup\{0\}\bigr)
. Assertion (i) follows from (
3
) for

\displaystyle M_{\mathrm{mid}}^{\mathrm{c}}(n,a,\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=\Bigl(\max\{M_{1,{\mathrm{mid}}}^{\mathrm{c}}(n,a,\varepsilon),M_{2,{\mathrm{mid}}}^{\mathrm{c}}(n,a,\varepsilon)\}\Bigr)\mathrm{Lenght}(\Gamma_{a,{\mathrm{mid}}}^{\varepsilon}),\;\mbox{where}

(3.62)

\displaystyle M_{1,{\mathrm{mid}}}^{\mathrm{c}}(n,a,\varepsilon)

\displaystyle:=\sup_{\lambda\in\mathrm{Range}(\Gamma_{a,{\mathrm{mid}}}^{\varepsilon})}\frac{\|R_{A}(\lambda)\|}{|\lambda|^{2n-3}},\;M_{2,{\mathrm{mid}}}^{\mathrm{c}}(n,a,\varepsilon):=\sup_{\lambda\in\mathrm{Range}(\Gamma_{a,{\mathrm{mid}}}^{\varepsilon})}\frac{1}{|\lambda|^{2n-1}}

Since the linear operator
A
is closed,
[
2
, Proposition 1.1.7]
gives

(3.63)

A\int_{\Gamma_{a,{\mathrm{mid}}}^{\varepsilon}}\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n}}R_{A}(\lambda)A^{n-1}w{\mathrm{d}}\lambda=\int_{\Gamma_{a,{\mathrm{mid}}}^{\varepsilon}}\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n}}AR_{A}(\lambda)A^{n-1}w{\mathrm{d}}\lambda.

Assertion (ii) follows from (
3
) and (
3.63
) for

\displaystyle M_{\mathrm{mid}}^{\mathrm{s}}(n,a,\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=\Bigl(\max\{M_{1,{\mathrm{mid}}}^{\mathrm{s}}(n,a,\varepsilon),M_{2,{\mathrm{mid}}}^{\mathrm{s}}(n,a,\varepsilon)\}\Bigr)\mathrm{Lenght}(\Gamma_{a,{\mathrm{mid}}}^{\varepsilon}),\;\mbox{where}

(3.64)

\displaystyle M_{1,{\mathrm{mid}}}^{\mathrm{s}}(n,a,\varepsilon)

\displaystyle:=\sup_{\lambda\in\mathrm{Range}(\Gamma_{a,{\mathrm{mid}}}^{\varepsilon})}\frac{\|R_{A}(\lambda)\|}{|\lambda|^{2n-2}},\;M_{2,{\mathrm{mid}}}^{\mathrm{s}}(n,a,\varepsilon):=\sup_{\lambda\in\mathrm{Range}(\Gamma_{a,{\mathrm{mid}}}^{\varepsilon})}\frac{1}{|\lambda|^{2n}}.

∎

In addition to the path
\Gamma_{a,{\mathrm{mid}}}^{\varepsilon}
introduced in (
3
), we introduce the path

(3.65)

\Gamma_{a,{\mathrm{out}}}^{\varepsilon}:\lambda=g_{0}(s)+\varepsilon+{\mathrm{i}}s,\;s\in(-\infty,-a]\cup[a,\infty).

To estimate integrals along the path
\Gamma_{a,{\mathrm{out}}}^{\varepsilon}
we need the elementary integral estimate (
3.71
) below.

Remark 3.14
.

Assume that the function
g_{0}
introduced in Hypothesis (H) is such that
g_{0},g_{0}^{\prime}\in L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}})
. We define

(3.66)

{\overline{s}}_{0}=\max\big\{2(\|g_{0}\|_{\infty}+\varepsilon_{0})\|g_{0}^{\prime}\|_{\infty},\|g_{0}\|_{\infty}+\varepsilon_{0}\big\}+1.

One can readily check that the function

(3.67)

s\to s^{2}+(g_{0}(s)+\varepsilon)^{2}\;\mbox{is increasing on}\;[{\overline{s}}_{0},\infty)\;\mbox{and decreasing on}\;(-\infty,-{\overline{s}}_{0}]

for any
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0})
. Moreover,

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{s^{2}+(g_{0}(s)+\varepsilon)^{2}}}{|s+(g_{0}(s)+\varepsilon)g_{0}^{\prime}(s)|}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{|s|+\|g_{0}\|_{\infty}+\varepsilon_{0}}{|s|-(\|g_{0}\|_{\infty}+\varepsilon_{0})\|g_{0}^{\prime}\|_{\infty}}\leq\frac{|s|+\|g_{0}\|_{\infty}+\varepsilon_{0}}{|s|-{\overline{s}}_{0}/2+1/2}\leq\frac{{\overline{s}}_{0}+\|g_{0}\|_{\infty}+\varepsilon_{0}}{{\overline{s}}_{0}/2+1/2}

(3.68)

\displaystyle\leq 2+\frac{2(\|g_{0}\|_{\infty}+\varepsilon_{0})}{{\overline{s}}_{0}}\leq 4,\;\mbox{whenever}\;|s|\geq{\overline{s}}_{0},\;\mbox{for any}\;\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}).

Fix a piecewise continuous function
h:[0,\infty)\to[0,\infty)
and
\eta>0
. Making the change of variables
\xi=\sqrt{s^{2}+(g_{0}(s)+\varepsilon)^{2}}
, from (
3.67
) and (
3.14
) we obtain that

\displaystyle\int_{{\overline{s}}_{0}}^{\infty}

\displaystyle\frac{h\big(\sqrt{s^{2}+(g_{0}(s)+\varepsilon)^{2}}\big){\mathrm{d}}s}{\big(s^{2}+(g_{0}(s)+\varepsilon)^{2}\big)^{\eta}}\leq 4\int_{{\overline{s}}_{0}}^{\infty}\frac{h\big(\sqrt{s^{2}+(g_{0}(s)+\varepsilon)^{2}}\big)}{\big(s^{2}+(g_{0}(s)+\varepsilon)^{2}\big)^{\eta}}\,\cdot\frac{s+(g_{0}(s)+\varepsilon)g_{0}^{\prime}(s)}{\sqrt{s^{2}+(g_{0}(s)+\varepsilon)^{2}}}{\mathrm{d}}s

(3.69)

\displaystyle=4\int_{\overline{\xi}_{\varepsilon,+}}^{\infty}\frac{h(\xi){\mathrm{d}}\xi}{\xi^{2\eta}}\leq 4\int_{1}^{\infty}\frac{h(\xi){\mathrm{d}}\xi}{\xi^{2\eta}},\;\mbox{where}\;\overline{\xi}_{\varepsilon,+}=\sqrt{{\overline{s}}_{0}^{2}+(g_{0}({\overline{s}}_{0})+\varepsilon)^{2}}\geq 1.

for any piecewise continuous function
h:[0,\infty)\to[0,\infty)
. Similarly,

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{-{\overline{s}}_{0}}

\displaystyle\frac{h\big(\sqrt{s^{2}+(g_{0}(s)+\varepsilon)^{2}}\big){\mathrm{d}}s}{\big(s^{2}+(g_{0}(s)+\varepsilon)^{2}\big)^{\eta}}\leq 4\int_{-\infty}^{-{\overline{s}}_{0}}\frac{h\big(\sqrt{s^{2}+(g_{0}(s)+\varepsilon)^{2}}\big)}{\big(s^{2}+(g_{0}(s)+\varepsilon)^{2}\big)^{\eta}}\,\cdot\Bigg(-\frac{s+(g_{0}(s)+\varepsilon)g_{0}^{\prime}(s)}{\sqrt{s^{2}+(g_{0}(s)+\varepsilon)^{2}}}\Bigg){\mathrm{d}}s

(3.70)

\displaystyle=4\int_{\overline{\xi}_{\varepsilon,-}}^{\infty}\frac{h(\xi){\mathrm{d}}\xi}{\xi^{2\eta}}\leq 4\int_{1}^{\infty}\frac{h(\xi){\mathrm{d}}\xi}{\xi^{2\eta}},\;\mbox{where}\;\overline{\xi}_{\varepsilon,-}=\sqrt{{\overline{s}}_{0}^{2}+(g_{0}(-{\overline{s}}_{0})+\varepsilon)^{2}}\geq 1.

Summarizing from (
3.14
) and (
3.14
) we infer that

(3.71)

\int_{|s|\geq{\overline{s}}_{0}}\frac{h\big(\sqrt{s^{2}+(g_{0}(s)+\varepsilon)^{2}}\big){\mathrm{d}}s}{\big(s^{2}+(g_{0}(s)+\varepsilon)^{2}\big)^{\eta}}\leq 8\int_{1}^{\infty}\frac{h(\xi){\mathrm{d}}\xi}{\xi^{2\eta}}

for any piecewise continuous function
h:[0,\infty)\to[0,\infty)
and any
\eta>0
.

Lemma 3.15
.

Assume Hypothesis (H) and, in addition, that
b:=\inf\limits_{s\in{\mathbb{R}}}g_{0}(s)>-\infty
,
g_{0}^{\prime}\in L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}})
,
\displaystyle\int_{1}^{\infty}\frac{h_{1}(s){\mathrm{d}}s}{s^{2n_{0}-1}}<\infty
. Then, for any
a>{\overline{a}}_{0}:={\overline{s}}_{0}+\max\limits_{1\leq k\leq m}|{\mathrm{Im}}\mu_{k}|
, with
{\overline{s}}_{0}
from (
3.66
),
t\geq 0
,
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0})
,
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
with
n\geq n_{0}
,
x\in\text{\rm{dom}}(A^{n})
,
w\in{\mathbb{W}}_{n-1}
,

(i)

The integral
\displaystyle\int_{\Gamma_{a,{\mathrm{out}}}^{\varepsilon}}\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n-1}}R_{A}(\lambda)A^{n}x{\mathrm{d}}\lambda
is absolutely convergent and

(3.72)

\Bigg\|\int_{\Gamma_{a,{\mathrm{out}}}^{\varepsilon}}\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n-1}}R_{A}(\lambda)A^{n}x{\mathrm{d}}\lambda\Bigg\|\leq 8\Bigg(\int_{1}^{\infty}\frac{h_{1}(\xi){\mathrm{d}}\xi}{\xi^{2n-1}}\Bigg)\Big(\|g_{0}^{\prime}\|_{\infty}+1\Big)\Big(\sup_{\xi\leq\gamma}h_{2}(\xi)\Big)e^{(\sup_{s\in{\mathbb{R}}}g_{0}(s)+\varepsilon)t}\|A^{n}x\|;

(ii)

The integral
\displaystyle\int_{\Gamma_{a,{\mathrm{out}}}^{\varepsilon}}\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n}}AR_{A}(\lambda)A^{n-1}w{\mathrm{d}}\lambda
is absolutely convergent and

(3.73)

\Bigg\|\int_{\Gamma_{a,{\mathrm{mid}}}^{\varepsilon}}\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n}}AR_{A}(\lambda)A^{n-1}w{\mathrm{d}}\lambda\Bigg\|\leq{\overline{M}}(n)e^{(\sup_{s\in{\mathbb{R}}}g_{0}(s)+\varepsilon)t}\|A^{n-1}w\|_{\mathbb{W}},

where

(3.74)

{\overline{M}}(n)=\big(\|g_{0}^{\prime}\|_{\infty}+1\big)\Bigg(32M_{1}(1+\omega)\Big(\sup_{\xi\leq\gamma}h_{2}(\xi)\Big)\int_{1}^{\infty}\frac{h_{1}(\xi){\mathrm{d}}\xi}{\xi^{2n-1}}+\frac{2M_{1}}{(2n-1)\omega}\Bigg).

Proof.

Fix
t\geq 0
,
x\in\text{\rm{dom}}(A^{n})
,
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0})
. By Hypothesis (H), (
3.65
) and (
3.71
) we obtain

\displaystyle\Bigg\|\int_{\Gamma_{a,{\mathrm{mid}}}^{\varepsilon}}

\displaystyle\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n-1}}R_{A}(\lambda)A^{n}x{\mathrm{d}}\lambda\Bigg\|\leq\int_{|s|\geq{\overline{s}}_{0}}\frac{|e^{(g_{0}(s)+\varepsilon+{\mathrm{i}}s)t}|\|A^{n}x\|}{|g_{0}(s)+\varepsilon+{\mathrm{i}}s|^{2n-1}}\big\|R_{A}\big(g_{0}(s)+\varepsilon+{\mathrm{i}}s\big)\big\||g_{0}^{\prime}(s)+{\mathrm{i}}|{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle\leq\|A^{n}x\|\big(\|g_{0}^{\prime}\|_{\infty}+1\big)\int_{|s|\geq{\overline{s}}_{0}}\frac{e^{(g_{0}(s)+\varepsilon)t}}{|g_{0}(s)+\varepsilon+{\mathrm{i}}s|^{2n-1}}h_{1}\big(|g_{0}(s)+\varepsilon+{\mathrm{i}}s|\big)h_{2}\big(g_{0}(s)+\varepsilon\big){\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle\leq\|A^{n}x\|\big(\|g_{0}^{\prime}\|_{\infty}+1\big)\Big(\sup_{\xi\leq\gamma}h_{2}(\xi)\Big)e^{(\sup_{s\in{\mathbb{R}}}g_{0}(s)+\varepsilon)t}\int_{|s|\geq{\overline{s}}_{0}}\frac{h_{1}\big(\sqrt{s^{2}+(g_{0}(s)+\varepsilon)^{2}}\big)}{\big(g_{0}(s)+\varepsilon+{\mathrm{i}}s\big)^{\frac{2n-1}{2}}}{\mathrm{d}}s

(3.75)

\displaystyle\leq 8\Bigg(\int_{1}^{\infty}\frac{h_{1}(\xi){\mathrm{d}}\xi}{\xi^{2n-1}}\Bigg)\Big(\|g_{0}^{\prime}\|_{\infty}+1\Big)\Big(\sup_{\xi\leq\gamma}h_{2}(\xi)\Big)e^{(\sup_{s\in{\mathbb{R}}}g_{0}(s)+\varepsilon)t}\|A^{n}x\|,

proving (i). Next, we fix
w\in{\mathbb{W}}_{n-1}
. Since
A^{n-1}w\in{\mathbb{W}}
from Lemma
3.7
we infer that

(3.76)

\|AR_{A}(\lambda)A^{n-1}w\|\leq\Big(c_{1}+c_{2}|\lambda|h_{1}(|\lambda|)h_{2}({\mathrm{Re}}\lambda)\Bigr)\|A^{n-1}w\|_{\mathbb{W}}

for any
\lambda\in\Omega_{0}\setminus{\mathbb{C}}_{\omega}^{+}
such that
|{\mathrm{Im}}\lambda|>\max\limits_{1\leq k\leq m}|{\mathrm{Im}}\mu_{k}|+1
, where
c_{1}=\frac{M_{1}}{\omega}
and
c_{2}=4M_{1}(1+\omega)
. From Hypothesis (H), (
3.65
), (
3.71
) and (
3.76
) it follows that

\displaystyle\Bigg\|\int_{\Gamma_{a,{\mathrm{mid}}}^{\varepsilon}}

\displaystyle\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n}}AR_{A}(\lambda)A^{n-1}w{\mathrm{d}}\lambda\Bigg\|\leq\int_{|s|\geq{\overline{s}}_{0}}\frac{|e^{(g_{0}(s)+\varepsilon+{\mathrm{i}}s)t}|}{|g_{0}(s)+\varepsilon+{\mathrm{i}}s|^{2n}}\big\|AR_{A}\big(g_{0}(s)+\varepsilon+{\mathrm{i}}s\big)A^{n-1}w\big\||g_{0}^{\prime}(s)+{\mathrm{i}}|{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle\leq\|A^{n-1}w\|\big(\|g_{0}^{\prime}\|_{\infty}+1\big)\Bigg(c_{2}\int_{|s|\geq{\overline{s}}_{0}}\frac{e^{(g_{0}(s)+\varepsilon)t}}{|g_{0}(s)+\varepsilon+{\mathrm{i}}s|^{2n-1}}h_{1}\big(|g_{0}(s)+\varepsilon+{\mathrm{i}}s|\big)h_{2}\big(g_{0}(s)+\varepsilon\big){\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+c_{1}\int_{|s|\geq{\overline{s}}_{0}}\frac{e^{(g_{0}(s)+\varepsilon)t}}{|g_{0}(s)+\varepsilon+{\mathrm{i}}s|^{2n}}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\|A^{n-1}w\|\big(\|g_{0}^{\prime}\|_{\infty}+1\big)e^{(\sup_{s\in{\mathbb{R}}}g_{0}(s)+\varepsilon)t}\Bigg(c_{2}\Big(\sup_{\xi\leq\gamma}h_{2}(\xi)\Big)\int_{|s|\geq{\overline{s}}_{0}}\frac{h_{1}\big(\sqrt{s^{2}+(g_{0}(s)+\varepsilon)^{2}}\big)}{\big(g_{0}(s)+\varepsilon+{\mathrm{i}}s\big)^{\frac{2n-1}{2}}}{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+2c_{1}\int_{|s|\geq 1}\frac{{\mathrm{d}}s}{|s|^{2n}}\Bigg)

(3.77)

\displaystyle\leq\|A^{n-1}w\|\big(\|g_{0}^{\prime}\|_{\infty}+1\big)e^{(\sup_{s\in{\mathbb{R}}}g_{0}(s)+\varepsilon)t}\Bigg(8c_{2}\Big(\sup_{\xi\leq\gamma}h_{2}(\xi)\Big)\int_{1}^{\infty}\frac{h_{1}(\xi){\mathrm{d}}\xi}{\xi^{2n-1}}+\frac{2c_{1}}{2n-1}\Bigg),

proving the lemma.
∎

To formulate the next theorem we introduce the notation

(3.78)

{\mathbb{N}}_{k}=\{n\in{\mathbb{N}}:n\geq k\}\;\mbox{for}\;k\in{\mathbb{N}}.

Theorem 3.16
.

Assume Hypothesis (H) and, in addition, that
b:=\inf\limits_{s\in{\mathbb{R}}}g_{0}(s)>-\infty
,
g_{0}^{\prime}\in L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}})
,
\displaystyle\int_{1}^{\infty}\frac{h_{1}(s){\mathrm{d}}s}{s^{2n_{0}-1}}<\infty
. Then,

(i)

There exists
M^{\mathrm{c}}:{\mathbb{N}}_{n_{0}}\times(0,\varepsilon_{0})\to(0,\infty)
and
{\mathscr{E}}^{\mathrm{c}}_{n}:{\mathbb{R}}\to\mathscr{B}(\text{\rm{dom}}(A^{n}),{\mathbb{X}})
such that

(3.79)

C(t)x=\sum\limits_{k=1}^{m}\sum_{j=1}^{N_{k}}p_{1,k,j}(t)e^{\mu_{k}t}(A-\mu_{k}^{2}I_{\mathbb{X}})^{j-1}P_{k}x+\chi_{[0,\infty)}(g_{0}(0))\displaystyle\sum\limits_{j=0}^{n-1}\frac{t^{2j}}{(2j)!}A^{j}x+{\mathscr{E}}^{\mathrm{c}}_{n}(t)x,

(3.80)

\|{\mathscr{E}}^{\mathrm{c}}_{n}(t)x\|\leq M^{\mathrm{c}}(n,\varepsilon)e^{(\sup_{s\in{\mathbb{R}}}g_{0}(s)+\varepsilon)t}\big(\|A^{n-1}x\|+\|A^{n}x\|\big)

for any
t\geq 0
,
x\in\text{\rm{dom}}(A^{n})
,
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0})
,
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
with
n\geq n_{0}
;

(ii)

There exists
M^{\mathrm{s}}:{\mathbb{N}}\times(0,\varepsilon_{0})\to(0,\infty)
and
{\mathscr{E}}^{\mathrm{s}}_{n}:{\mathbb{R}}\to\mathscr{B}(W_{n-1},{\mathbb{X}})
such that

(3.81)

S(t)w=\sum\limits_{k=1}^{m}\sum_{j=1}^{N_{k}}p_{0,k,j}(t)e^{\mu_{k}t}(A-\mu_{k}^{2}I_{\mathbb{X}})^{j-1}P_{k}w+\chi_{[0,\infty)}(g_{0}(0))\sum\limits_{j=0}^{n-1}\frac{t^{2j+1}}{(2j+1)!}A^{j}w+{\mathscr{E}}^{\mathrm{s}}_{n}(t)w

(3.82)

\|{\mathscr{E}}^{\mathrm{s}}_{n}(t)w\|\leq M^{\mathrm{s}}(n,\varepsilon)e^{(\sup_{s\in{\mathbb{R}}}g_{0}(s)+\varepsilon)t}\|A^{n-1}w\|_{\mathbb{W}}

for any
t\geq 0
,
w\in{\mathbb{W}}_{n-1}
,
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0})
,
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
with
n\geq n_{0}
.

We recall the definition of
n_{0}
in Hypothesis (H)(iv), the projection
P_{k}
and numbers
N_{k}\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
k=1,\dots,m
, in Lemma
3.3
, and of the polynomial
p_{\kappa,k,j}
,
\kappa=0,1
, in (
3.28
).

Proof.

The theorem follows from Theorem
3.11
, Theorem
3.12
, Lemma
3.13
and Lemma
3.15
.
∎

4.
Extensions across the essential spectrum

In this section we aim to refine the representations given in Section
3
, see, in particular, Theorem
3.16
, for the cosine and sine operator families. To achieve this goal we need to study the
resonances
of the generator of the cosine family. In the case of Schrödinger operators considered on
L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{m})
, with
m
being an odd positive integer, it is possible, see, e.g.,
[
15
]
to extend the operator-valued function
R_{A}(\cdot)_{|L^{2}_{\mathrm{comp}}({\mathbb{R}}^{m})}
to a meromorphic function. The extension is defined on a set that contains the essential spectrum of
A
and takes values in the set of continuous, linear operators
{\mathscr{C}}{\mathscr{L}}\big(L^{2}_{\mathrm{comp}}({\mathbb{R}}^{m}),L^{2}_{\mathrm{loc}}({\mathbb{R}}^{m})\big)
. We refer to
[
15
]
for a very detailed discussion in this important special case.

4.1.
General Frechet Space Framework

To proceed in our general case when
A
is the generator of a cosine family on a Banach space
{\mathbb{X}}
,
we need to find a subspace of
{\mathbb{X}}
that plays the role of the space
L^{2}_{\mathrm{comp}}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})
and a Frechet space
{\mathbb{Y}}
that contains the original Banach space to which we can extend the generator
A
in a natural way, and
{\mathbb{Y}}
will be playing the role of
L^{2}_{\mathrm{loc}}({\mathbb{R}}^{m})
.

Hypothesis (Q).
We assume that
{\mathbb{X}}
is a Banach space,
A
is the generator of a cosine family on
{\mathbb{X}}
and,
{\mathbb{Y}}
is a Frechet space such that
{\mathbb{X}}\hookrightarrow{\mathbb{Y}}
,
Q_{n}:{\mathbb{Y}}\to{\mathbb{X}}
,
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
, are linear operators such that

(
Q_{1}
)

{Q_{n}}_{|{\mathbb{X}}}\in\mathscr{B}({\mathbb{X}})
for any
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
;

(
Q_{2}
)

Q_{n}x\xrightarrow{\hskip 4.09723pt{\mathbb{X}}\hskip 4.09723pt}x
as
n\to\infty
for any
x\in{\mathbb{X}}
;

(
Q_{3}
)

Q_{n}Q_{k}=Q_{k}Q_{n}=Q_{n}
for any
k,n\in{\mathbb{N}}
with
k\geq n+1
;

(
Q_{4}
)

Q_{n}\big(\text{\rm{dom}}(A)\big)\subseteq\text{\rm{dom}}(A)
and
({Q_{n}}_{|{\mathbb{X}}})^{*}\big(\text{\rm{dom}}(A^{*})\big)\subseteq\text{\rm{dom}}(A^{*})
;

(
Q_{5}
)

A\big(\text{\rm{dom}}(A)\cap{\mathrm{Ker}}(Q_{n}-I_{\mathbb{Y}})\big)\subseteq{\mathrm{Ker}}(Q_{n}-I_{\mathbb{Y}})
for any
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
;

(
Q_{6}
)

Q_{n}AQ_{k}x=Q_{n}Ax
for any
x\in\text{\rm{dom}}(A)
,
k,n\in{\mathbb{N}}
with
k\geq n+1
;

(
Q_{7}
)

For any
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
the linear operator
{\mathscr{G}}_{n}:{\rm Graph}(A)\to{\rm Graph}(A)
defined by
{\mathscr{G}}_{n}(x,Ax)=(Q_{n}x,AQ_{n}x)
is continuous when
{\rm Graph}(A)
is endowed with the
{\mathbb{Y}}\times{\mathbb{Y}}
topology;

(
Q_{8}
)

The topology on
{\mathbb{Y}}
is equivalent to the topology induced by the family of seminorms
\{{\mathfrak{q}}_{n}\}_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
defined by
{\mathfrak{q}}_{n}:{\mathbb{Y}}\to[0,\infty)
,
{\mathfrak{q}}_{n}(y):=\|Q_{n}y\|_{\mathbb{X}}
,
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
.

Example 4.1
.

If
{\mathbb{X}}=L^{p}({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{C}}^{m})
and
A=\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{k}B_{j}(x)\partial_{x}^{j}
, where
B_{j}\in{\mathscr{C}}_{\mathrm{b}}({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{C}}^{m\times m})
,
j=0,1,\dots,m
. In this case
\text{\rm{dom}}(A)=W^{p,k}({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{C}}^{m})
and
{\mathbb{Y}}=L_{\mathrm{loc}}^{p}({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{C}}^{m})
, endowed with their canonical topologies. Let
\varphi_{n}\in{\mathscr{C}}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}})
be such that
0\leq\varphi_{n}\leq 1
,
\sup\limits_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}\|\varphi_{n}^{\prime}\|_{\infty}<\infty
,
\varphi_{n}(t)=1
for any
t\in[-n,n]
,
\varphi_{n}(t)=0
, whenever
|t|\geq n+1
. We define the linear operator
Q_{n}:L^{p}_{\mathrm{loc}}({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{C}}^{m})\to L^{p}({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{C}}^{m})
by
Q_{n}f=\varphi_{n}f
. One can readily check that

(4.1)

(\varphi_{n}f)^{(j)}=\sum_{\ell=0}^{j}\left(\begin{matrix}j\\
\ell\end{matrix}\right)\varphi^{(j-\ell)}f^{(\ell)}\;\mbox{for any}\;f\in W^{p,k}({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{C}}^{m}),n\in{\mathbb{N}},j=1,\dots,k.

(4.2)

{\mathrm{Ker}}(Q_{n}-I)=\{f\in L^{p}({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{C}}^{m}):f_{|{\mathbb{R}}\setminus[-n,n]}=0\,\mathrm{a.e}\}\;\mbox{for any}\;n\in{\mathbb{N}}.

Using Lebesgue Dominated Convergence Theorem, along with (
4.1
) and (
4.2
), we can see that Hypothesis (Q) holds in this case.

Our first task is to describe immediate consequences of Hypothesis (Q). We recall that a subset
{\mathbb{L}}\subset{\mathbb{Y}}
is
bounded
provided that

(4.3)

\sup_{y\in{\mathbb{L}}}{\mathfrak{q}}_{k}(y)<\infty\;\mbox{for any}\;k\in{\mathbb{N}}.

The space
{\mathbb{Y}}^{*}
is a locally convex space when endowed by the topology induced by the family of seminorms
\{{\mathfrak{p}}_{{\mathbb{L}},*}:{\mathbb{L}}\;\mbox{is a bounded subset of}\;{\mathbb{Y}}\}
, where

(4.4)

{\mathfrak{p}}_{{\mathbb{L}},*}:{\mathbb{Y}}^{*}\to[0,\infty)\;\mbox{is defined by}\;{\mathfrak{p}}_{{\mathbb{L}},*}(y^{*})=\sup_{y\in{\mathbb{L}}}|\langle y,y^{*}\rangle|.

In what follows an essential role is played by the set

(4.5)

{\mathbb{X}}_{\infty}=\bigcup\limits_{n=1}^{\infty}{\mathrm{Ker}}(Q_{n}-I_{\mathbb{Y}}).

Lemma 4.2
.

Assume Hypothesis (Q). Then, the following assertions hold true:

(i)

{\mathrm{Ker}}(Q_{n}-I_{\mathbb{Y}})={\mathrm{Ker}}\big((Q_{n})_{|{\mathbb{X}}}-I_{\mathbb{X}}\big)\subset{\mathbb{X}}
is a closed subspace in the
\|\cdot\|_{\mathbb{X}}
norm for any
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
;

(ii)

{\mathrm{Ker}}(Q_{n}-I_{\mathbb{Y}})\subseteq{\rm Range}(Q_{n})\subseteq{\mathrm{Ker}}(Q_{n+1}-I_{\mathbb{Y}})\subset{\mathbb{X}}
for any
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
;

(iii)

Q_{k}AQ_{n}x=AQ_{n}x
for any
x\in\text{\rm{dom}}(A)
,
k,n\in{\mathbb{N}}
with
k\geq n+1
;

(iv)

{\mathbb{X}}_{\infty}
is a dense subspace of
{\mathbb{X}}
in the
\|\cdot\|_{\mathbb{X}}
norm, invariant under
A
;

(v)

Q_{n}y\xrightarrow{\hskip 4.09723pt{\mathbb{Y}}\hskip 4.09723pt}y
as
n\to\infty
for any
y\in{\mathbb{Y}}
;

(vi)

Q_{k}\in{\mathscr{C}}{\mathscr{L}}({\mathbb{Y}},{\mathbb{X}})
for any
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
;

(vii)

Q_{k}^{*}\in{\mathscr{C}}{\mathscr{L}}({\mathbb{X}}^{*},{\mathbb{Y}}^{*})
for any
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
;

(viii)

\text{\rm{dom}}(A)
is a dense subspace of
{\mathbb{Y}}
in the
{\mathbb{Y}}
-topology;

(ix)

\text{\rm{dom}}(A)\cap{\mathbb{X}}_{\infty}
is dense in
{\mathbb{X}}
in the
\|\cdot\|_{\mathbb{X}}
norm;

(x)

If, in addition, we assume that
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
and
Q_{j}(\text{\rm{dom}}(A^{n}))\subseteq\text{\rm{dom}}(A^{n})
for any
j\in{\mathbb{N}}
, then
\text{\rm{dom}}(A^{n-1})\cap{\mathrm{Ker}}(Q_{j}-I_{\mathbb{Y}})
is contained in the closure of
\text{\rm{dom}}(A^{n})\cap{\mathrm{Ker}}(Q_{j+1}-I_{\mathbb{Y}})
in the
\|\cdot\|_{\text{\rm{dom}}(A^{n-1})}
norm for any
j\in{\mathbb{N}}
.

Proof.

Assertion (i) follows shortly from (
Q_{1}
) and since
{\rm Range}(Q_{n})\subset{\mathbb{X}}
for any
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
. Obviously,
{\mathrm{Ker}}(Q_{n}-I_{\mathbb{Y}})\subseteq{\rm Range}(Q_{n})
. Moreover, from (
Q_{3}
) one can readily check that
{\rm Range}(Q_{n})\subseteq{\mathrm{Ker}}(Q_{n+1}-I_{\mathbb{Y}})
for any
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
, proving assertion (ii).

Next, we fix
x\in\text{\rm{dom}}(A)
. From (ii) and (
Q_{4}
) it follows that
Q_{n}x\in\text{\rm{dom}}(A)\cap{\mathrm{Ker}}(Q_{n+1}-I_{\mathbb{Y}})
for any
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
. From (
Q_{5}
) we infer that
AQ_{n}x\in{\mathrm{Ker}}(Q_{n+1}-I_{\mathbb{Y}})\subseteq{\mathrm{Ker}}(Q_{k}-I_{\mathbb{Y}})
for
k,n\in{\mathbb{N}}
with
k\geq n+1
, which proves assertion (iii).
From (ii) one can readily check that
{\mathbb{X}}_{\infty}
is a subspace of
{\mathbb{X}}
. Assertion (iv) follows from (
Q_{2}
), (
Q_{5}
) and since
Q_{n}x\in{\rm Range}(Q_{n})\subseteq{\mathrm{Ker}}(Q_{n+1}-I_{\mathbb{Y}})\subset{\mathbb{X}}_{\infty}
for any
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
x\in{\mathbb{X}}
by (ii).

From (
Q_{3}
) we know that
Q_{\ell}Q_{n}y=Q_{\ell}y
for any
y\in{\mathbb{Y}}
and any
n,\ell\in{\mathbb{N}}
with
n\geq\ell
. It follows that
{\mathfrak{q}}_{\ell}(Q_{n}y-y)\to 0
as
n\to\infty
for any
\ell\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
y\in{\mathbb{Y}}
, proving assertion (v). Assertion (vi) follows immediately from (
Q_{8}
). Next, fix
\{x_{n}^{*}\}_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
a sequence of elements of
{\mathbb{X}}^{*}
such that
x_{n}^{*}\xrightarrow{\hskip 4.09723pt{\mathbb{X}}^{*}\hskip 4.09723pt}0
as
n\to\infty
. Then,

(4.6)

{\mathfrak{p}}_{{\mathbb{L}},*}(Q_{k}^{*}x_{n}^{*})=\sup_{y\in{\mathbb{L}}}|\langle y,Q_{k}^{*}x_{n}^{*}\rangle|=\sup_{y\in{\mathbb{L}}}|\langle Q_{k}y,x_{n}^{*}\rangle|\leq\big(\sup_{y\in{\mathbb{L}}}\|Q_{k}y\|\big)\|x_{n}^{*}\|=\big(\sup_{y\in{\mathbb{L}}}{\mathfrak{q}}_{k}(y)\big)\|x_{n}^{*}\|

for any
n,k\in{\mathbb{N}}
and any bounded subset
{\mathbb{L}}
of
{\mathbb{Y}}
. Assertion (vii) follows from (
4.3
) and (
4.6
).

Next, we fix
y\in{\mathbb{Y}}
. Since
Q_{n}y\in{\mathbb{X}}
for any
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
and
\text{\rm{dom}}(A)
is a dense subspace of
{\mathbb{X}}
in the
\|\cdot\|_{\mathbb{X}}
norm, we infer that for any
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
there exists
x_{n}\in\text{\rm{dom}}(A)
such that
\|x_{n}-Q_{n}y\|\leq\frac{1}{n}
. It follows that
x_{n}-Q_{n}y\xrightarrow{\hskip 4.09723pt{\mathbb{X}}\hskip 4.09723pt}0
as
n\to\infty
. Since
{\mathbb{X}}\hookrightarrow{\mathbb{Y}}
, from (v) we conclude that
x_{n}\xrightarrow{\hskip 4.09723pt{\mathbb{Y}}\hskip 4.09723pt}y
as
n\to\infty
, proving assertion (viii).

Finally, from (
Q_{1}
) and the Uniform Boundedness Principle we immediately infer that

(4.7)

q_{*}:=\sup_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}\|{Q_{n}}_{|{\mathbb{X}}}\|<\infty.

Fix
x\in{\mathbb{X}}
. Since
\text{\rm{dom}}(A)
is a dense subspace of
{\mathbb{X}}
in the
\|\cdot\|_{\mathbb{X}}
norm, there exists
\{x_{n}\}_{n\geq 1}
a sequence of elements of
\text{\rm{dom}}(A)
such that
x_{n}\xrightarrow{\hskip 4.09723pt{\mathbb{X}}\hskip 4.09723pt}x
as
n\to\infty
. From (
Q_{4}
) we have
Q_{n}x_{n}\in\text{\rm{dom}}(A)\cap{\rm Range}(Q_{n})\subseteq\text{\rm{dom}}(A)\cap{\mathbb{X}}_{\infty}
for any
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
. Using (
4.7
) we have

(4.8)

\|Q_{n}x_{n}-x\|\leq\|Q_{n}x_{n}-Q_{n}x\|+\|Q_{n}x-x\|\leq q_{*}\|x_{n}-x\|+\|Q_{n}x-x\|\;\mbox{for any}\;n\in{\mathbb{N}}.

Using (
Q_{1}
) and (
4.8
) we conclude that
Q_{n}x_{n}\xrightarrow{\hskip 4.09723pt{\mathbb{X}}\hskip 4.09723pt}x
as
n\to\infty
, proving assertion (ix).

Finally, we fix
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
and assume that
Q_{j}(\text{\rm{dom}}(A^{n}))\subseteq\text{\rm{dom}}(A^{n})
for any
j\in{\mathbb{N}}
. Let
{K}_{i,j}:\text{\rm{dom}}(A^{i})\to{\mathbb{X}}
be the linear operator defined by

(4.9)

{K}_{i,j}x=A^{i}Q_{j}x-Q_{j}A^{i}x\;\mbox{for}\;j\in{\mathbb{N}},i=0,\dots,n.

Let
\{x_{k}\}_{k\geq 1}
be a sequence of elements of
\text{\rm{dom}}(A^{i})
,
x\in\text{\rm{dom}}(A^{i})
and
{\widetilde{x}}\in{\mathbb{X}}
such that

(4.10)

x_{k}\xrightarrow{\hskip 4.09723pt\text{\rm{dom}}(A^{i})\hskip 4.09723pt}x\;\mbox{and}\;{K}_{i,j}x_{k}\xrightarrow{\hskip 4.09723pt{\mathbb{X}}\hskip 4.09723pt}{\widetilde{x}}\;\mbox{as}\;k\to\infty.

From (
Q_{1}
) and (
4.10
) we obtain

(4.11)

Q_{j}x_{k}\xrightarrow{\hskip 4.09723pt{\mathbb{X}}\hskip 4.09723pt}Q_{j}x\;\mbox{and}\;Q_{j}A^{i}x_{k}\xrightarrow{\hskip 4.09723pt{\mathbb{X}}\hskip 4.09723pt}Q_{j}A^{i}x\;\mbox{as}\;k\to\infty.

From (
4.10
) and (
4.11
) it follows that
A^{i}Q_{j}x_{k}\xrightarrow{\hskip 4.09723pt{\mathbb{X}}\hskip 4.09723pt}Q_{j}A^{i}x+{\widetilde{x}}
as
k\to\infty
. We recall that
A^{i}
is a closed linear operator since
A
is closed with non-empty resolvent set. Hence,
{K}_{i,j}
is a closed linear operator. Thus, by the Closed Graph Theorem, we conclude that

(4.12)

{K}_{i,j}\in\mathscr{B}(\text{\rm{dom}}(A^{i}),{\mathbb{X}})\;\mbox{for any}\;j\in{\mathbb{N}},i=0,\dots,n.

Next, we fix
j\in{\mathbb{N}}
and
x\in\text{\rm{dom}}(A^{n-1})\cap{\mathrm{Ker}}(Q_{j}-I_{\mathbb{Y}})
. We define

(4.13)

{\widetilde{x}}_{k}=k\int_{0}^{1/k}T(s)x{\mathrm{d}}s,\;\;k\in{\mathbb{N}},

where
\{T(z)\}_{z\in{\mathbb{C}}_{0}^{+}}
is the analytic semigroup generated by
A
, given by (
2.18
). From
[
2
, Proposition 1.1.7]
we have

(4.14)

{\widetilde{x}}_{k}\in\text{\rm{dom}}(A^{i})\;\mbox{and}\;A^{i}{\widetilde{x}}_{k}=k\int_{0}^{1/k}T(s)A^{i}x{\mathrm{d}}s\;\mbox{for any}\;k\in{\mathbb{N}},i=0,\dots,n-1.

It follows that
A^{i}{\widetilde{x}}_{k}\xrightarrow{\hskip 4.09723pt{\mathbb{X}}\hskip 4.09723pt}A^{i}x
as
k\to\infty
, for any
i=0,\dots,n-1
, hence

(4.15)

{\widetilde{x}}_{k}\xrightarrow{\hskip 4.09723pt\text{\rm{dom}}(A^{n-1})\hskip 4.09723pt}A^{i}x\;\mbox{as}\;k\to\infty.

Moreover, from (
4.14
) we infer that
{\widetilde{x}}_{k}\in\text{\rm{dom}}(A^{n})
for any
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
. Since
Q_{j}(\text{\rm{dom}}(A^{n}))\subseteq\text{\rm{dom}}(A^{n})
, from (ii) we infer that
Q_{j}{\widetilde{x}}_{k}\in\text{\rm{dom}}(A^{n})\cap{\mathrm{Ker}}(Q_{j+1}-I_{\mathbb{Y}})
for any
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
. From (
Q_{1}
), (
4.12
) and (
4.15
) we obtain that

(4.16)

A^{i}Q_{j}{\widetilde{x}}_{k}={K}_{i,j}{\widetilde{x}}_{k}+Q_{j}A^{i}{\widetilde{x}}_{k}\xrightarrow{\hskip 4.09723pt{\mathbb{X}}\hskip 4.09723pt}{K}_{i,j}x+Q_{j}A^{i}x=A^{i}Q_{j}x=A^{i}x\;\mbox{as}\;k\to\infty.

for any
i=0,\dots,n
. We conclude that
Q_{j}{\widetilde{x}}_{k}\xrightarrow{\hskip 4.09723pt\text{\rm{dom}}(A^{n-1})\hskip 4.09723pt}x
as
k\to\infty
, proving the lemma.
∎

4.2.
The closed extension of the generator
A
to the Frechet Space
{\mathbb{Y}}

Next, we aim to show that the linear operator
A:\text{\rm{dom}}(A)\subseteq{\mathbb{X}}\to{\mathbb{X}}
can be extended to a closed linear operator
{\widetilde{A}}
acting in the Frechet space
{\mathbb{Y}}
and that the extension preserves at least some of the properties of
A
associated
with the family of operators
\{Q_{n}\}_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
, in particular the localization property (
Q_{6}
). We denote by
A_{\mathbb{Y}}:\text{\rm{dom}}(A)\subset{\mathbb{Y}}\to{\mathbb{Y}}
the linear operator defined by
A_{\mathbb{Y}}y=Ay
for
y\in\text{\rm{dom}}(A)\subseteq{\mathbb{X}}\hookrightarrow{\mathbb{Y}}
. We will show that the linear operator
A_{\mathbb{Y}}
is
closable
in the
{\mathbb{Y}}
topology. First, we recall the following classical result of F. E. Browder.

Lemma 4.3
.

(
[
5
, Lemma 2.1(iii)]
)
If
{\mathbb{Y}}
is a Frechet space,
B:\text{\rm{dom}}(B)\subseteq{\mathbb{Y}}\to{\mathbb{Y}}
is a linear operator,
\text{\rm{dom}}(B)
is dense in
{\mathbb{Y}}
, then
B
is closable if and only if
\text{\rm{dom}}(B^{*})
is dense in the weak
*
topology (denoted
\sigma({\mathbb{Y}}^{*},{\mathbb{Y}})
) on
{\mathbb{Y}}^{*}
.

Lemma 4.4
.

Assume Hypothesis (Q). Then the following assertions hold true:

(i)

Q_{n}^{*}\big(\text{\rm{dom}}(A^{*})\big)\subseteq\text{\rm{dom}}(A_{\mathbb{Y}}^{*})
for any
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
;

(ii)

A_{\mathbb{Y}}
is closable.

Proof.

(i) Fix
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
y\in\text{\rm{dom}}(A)
and
x^{*}\in\text{\rm{dom}}(A^{*})
. From (
Q_{4}
) and (
Q_{6}
) we have

(4.17)

Q_{n+1}y\in\text{\rm{dom}}(A),(Q_{n})^{*}_{|{\mathbb{X}}}x^{*}\in\text{\rm{dom}}(A^{*}),Q_{n}Ay=(Q_{n})_{|{\mathbb{X}}}AQ_{n+1}y.

Moreover, one can readily check that

\displaystyle\langle A_{\mathbb{Y}}y,Q_{n}^{*}x^{*}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle Ay,Q_{n}^{*}x^{*}\rangle=\langle Q_{n}Ay,x^{*}\rangle=\langle{Q_{n}}_{|{\mathbb{X}}}AQ_{n+1}y,x^{*}\rangle

(4.18)

\displaystyle=\langle AQ_{n+1}y,{Q_{n}}_{|{\mathbb{X}}}^{*}x^{*}\rangle=\langle Q_{n+1}y,A^{*}{Q_{n}}_{|{\mathbb{X}}}^{*}x^{*}\rangle.

Since
A^{*}{Q_{n}}_{|{\mathbb{X}}}^{*}x^{*}\in{\mathbb{X}}^{*}
and
Q_{n+1}\in{\mathscr{C}}{\mathscr{L}}({\mathbb{Y}},{\mathbb{X}})
by Lemma
4.2
(vi) it follows that the functional

(4.19)

y\to\big\langle Q_{n+1}y,A^{*}{Q_{n}}_{|{\mathbb{X}}}^{*}x^{*}\big\rangle:{\mathbb{Y}}\to{\mathbb{C}}\;\mbox{is continuous}.

Hence,
Q_{n}x^{*}\in\text{\rm{dom}}(A_{\mathbb{Y}}^{*})
, proving (i).

We use Lemma
4.3
to show that
\text{\rm{dom}}(A_{\mathbb{Y}}^{*})
is a dense subspace of
{\mathbb{Y}}^{*}
in the
\sigma({\mathbb{Y}}^{*},{\mathbb{Y}})
topology. Fix
y^{*}\in{\mathbb{Y}}^{*}
. Since
{\mathbb{X}}\hookrightarrow{\mathbb{Y}}
one can readily check that
y^{*}_{|{\mathbb{X}}}\in{\mathbb{X}}^{*}
. Next, we define the linear functionals
f_{k}^{*}:{\mathbb{X}}\to{\mathbb{C}}
,
{\widetilde{f}}_{k}^{*}:{\mathbb{Y}}\to{\mathbb{C}}
by

(4.20)

f_{k}^{*}(x)=\langle(Q_{k})_{|{\mathbb{X}}}x,y^{*}\rangle,\;{\widetilde{f}}_{k}^{*}(y)=\langle Q_{k}y,y^{*}\rangle.

Since
(Q_{k})_{|{\mathbb{X}}}\in\mathscr{B}({\mathbb{X}})
by (
Q_{1}
) and
Q_{k}\in{\mathscr{C}}{\mathscr{L}}({\mathbb{Y}},{\mathbb{X}})
by Lemma
4.2
(ii) we infer that
f_{k}^{*}\in{\mathbb{X}}^{*}
and
{\widetilde{f}}_{k}^{*}\in{\mathbb{Y}}^{*}
for any
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
. From (
Q_{3}
) and (
4.20
) we have

(4.21)

\langle y,Q_{k+1}^{*}f_{k}^{*}\rangle=\langle Q_{k+1}y,f_{k}^{*}\rangle=\langle Q_{k}Q_{k+1}y,y^{*}\rangle=\langle Q_{k}y,y^{*}\rangle=\langle y,{\widetilde{f}}_{k}^{*}\rangle\;\mbox{for any}\;y\in{\mathbb{Y}},k\in{\mathbb{N}},

which implies that

(4.22)

Q_{k+1}^{*}f_{k}^{*}={\widetilde{f}}_{k}^{*}\;\mbox{for any}\;k\in{\mathbb{N}}.

Moreover, from Lemma
4.2
(v) it follows that

(4.23)

\langle y,{\widetilde{f}}_{k}^{*}\rangle=\langle Q_{k}y,y^{*}\rangle\longrightarrow\langle y,y^{*}\rangle\;\mbox{as}\;k\to\infty\;\mbox{for any}\;y\in{\mathbb{Y}}.

Hence,

(4.24)

{\widetilde{f}}_{k}^{*}\xrightarrow{\hskip 4.09723pt\sigma({\mathbb{Y}}^{*},{\mathbb{Y}})\hskip 4.09723pt}y^{*}\;\mbox{as}\;k\to\infty.

Next, we prove that the sequence
\{{\widetilde{f}}_{k}^{*}\}_{k\in{\mathbb{N}}}
belongs to the closure of
\text{\rm{dom}}(A_{\mathbb{Y}}^{*})
in
\sigma({\mathbb{Y}}^{*},{\mathbb{Y}})
-topology. Fix
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
. Since
A
is a closed, densely defined linear operator on
{\mathbb{X}}
, from Lemma
4.3
we infer that
\text{\rm{dom}}(A^{*})
is dense in
{\mathbb{X}}^{*}
in the weak
*
topology (
\sigma({\mathbb{X}}^{*},{\mathbb{X}})
). Thus, there exists a sequence
\{x_{n}^{*}\}_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
of vectors of
\text{\rm{dom}}(A^{*})
such that
x_{n}^{*}\xrightarrow{\hskip 4.09723pt\sigma({\mathbb{X}}^{*},{\mathbb{X}})\hskip 4.09723pt}f_{k}^{*}
as
n\to\infty
. Using (
4.22
) we obtain that

(4.25)

\langle y,Q_{k+1}^{*}x_{n}^{*}\rangle=\langle Q_{k+1}y,x_{n}^{*}\rangle\longrightarrow\langle Q_{k+1}y,f_{k}^{*}\rangle=\langle y,Q_{k+1}^{*}f_{k}^{*}\rangle=\langle y,{\widetilde{f}}_{k}^{*}\rangle\;\mbox{as}\;k\to\infty\;\mbox{for any}\;y\in{\mathbb{Y}}.

We conclude that
Q_{k+1}^{*}x_{n}^{*}\xrightarrow{\hskip 4.09723pt\sigma({\mathbb{Y}}^{*},{\mathbb{Y}})\hskip 4.09723pt}{\widetilde{f}}_{k}^{*}
as
n\to\infty
. Since
x_{n}^{*}\in\text{\rm{dom}}(A^{*})
for any
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
, from assertion (i) we infer that
Q_{k+1}^{*}x_{n}^{*}\in\text{\rm{dom}}(A_{\mathbb{Y}}^{*})
for any
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
, which shows that

(4.26)

{\widetilde{f}}_{k}^{*}\in\overline{\text{\rm{dom}}(A_{\mathbb{Y}}^{*})}\;\mbox{in the}\;\sigma({\mathbb{Y}}^{*},{\mathbb{Y}})\;\mbox{topology for any}\;k\in{\mathbb{N}}.

From (
4.24
) and (
4.26
) it follows that
y^{*}
belongs to the closure of
\text{\rm{dom}}(A_{\mathbb{Y}}^{*})
in the
\sigma({\mathbb{Y}}^{*},{\mathbb{Y}})
topology, and thus
\text{\rm{dom}}(A_{\mathbb{Y}}^{*})
is a dense subspace of
{\mathbb{Y}}^{*}
in the
\sigma({\mathbb{Y}}^{*},{\mathbb{Y}})
topology. From Lemma
4.3
we infer that
A_{\mathbb{Y}}
is closable in
{\mathbb{Y}}
, proving the lemma.
∎

We denote by
{\widetilde{A}}:\text{\rm{dom}}({\widetilde{A}})\subseteq{\mathbb{Y}}\to{\mathbb{Y}}
the closure of the closable operator
A_{\mathbb{Y}}
. Then,
{\rm Graph}({\widetilde{A}})
is the closure of
{\rm Graph}(A)
in the
{\mathbb{Y}}\times{\mathbb{Y}}
topology. Next, we study the properties of
{\widetilde{A}}
.

Remark 4.5
.

Under Hypothesis (Q), the space
\text{\rm{dom}}({\widetilde{A}})
is a Frechet when endowed with the family of seminorms
\{{\widetilde{\mathfrak{p}}}_{n}\}_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
defined by

(4.27)

{\widetilde{\mathfrak{p}}}_{n}:\text{\rm{dom}}({\widetilde{A}})\to[0,\infty),\;\;{\widetilde{\mathfrak{p}}}(y)=\|Q_{n}y\|_{\mathbb{X}}+\|Q_{n}{\widetilde{A}}y\|_{\mathbb{X}},n\in{\mathbb{N}}.

Lemma 4.6
.

Assume Hypothesis (Q). Then, the following assertions hold true:

(i)

For any
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
the linear operator
(Q_{n})_{|\text{\rm{dom}}(A)}:\text{\rm{dom}}(A)\to\text{\rm{dom}}(A)
is continuous when we use the topology induced by the family of seminorms
\{{\widetilde{\mathfrak{p}}}_{n}\}_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
on the domain and the
\|\cdot\|_{\text{\rm{dom}}(A)}
norm on the image space of the operator;

(ii)

\text{\rm{dom}}(A)
is a dense subspace of
\text{\rm{dom}}({\widetilde{A}})
in the topology induced by the family of seminorms
\{{\widetilde{\mathfrak{p}}}_{n}\}_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
.

Proof.

Let
\{x_{k}\}_{k\in{\mathbb{N}}}
be a sequence of vectors of
\text{\rm{dom}}(A)
such that
{\widetilde{\mathfrak{p}}}_{m}(x_{k})\longrightarrow 0
as
k\to\infty
for any
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
. From (
Q_{8}
) we have
x_{k}\xrightarrow{\hskip 4.09723pt{\mathbb{Y}}\hskip 4.09723pt}0
and
Ax_{k}\xrightarrow{\hskip 4.09723pt{\mathbb{Y}}\hskip 4.09723pt}0
as
k\to\infty
. From (
Q_{7}
) we obtain that

(4.28)

(Q_{n}x_{k},AQ_{n}x_{k})={\mathscr{G}}_{n}(x_{k},Ax_{k})\xrightarrow{\hskip 4.09723pt{\mathbb{Y}}\times{\mathbb{Y}}\hskip 4.09723pt}(0,0)\;\mbox{as}\;k\to\infty\;\mbox{for any}\;n\in{\mathbb{N}}.

From Lemma
4.2
(iii) and (
4.28
) it follows that

(4.29)

AQ_{n}x_{k}=Q_{n+1}AQ_{n}x_{k}\xrightarrow{\hskip 4.09723pt{\mathbb{X}}\hskip 4.09723pt}0\;\mbox{as}\;k\to\infty\;\mbox{for any}\;n\in{\mathbb{N}}.

Moreover, since
x_{k}\xrightarrow{\hskip 4.09723pt{\mathbb{Y}}\hskip 4.09723pt}0
as
k\to\infty
from (
Q_{8}
) we have
Q_{n}x_{k}\xrightarrow{\hskip 4.09723pt{\mathbb{X}}\hskip 4.09723pt}0
as
k\to\infty
. Using (
4.29
) we conclude that

(4.30)

\|Q_{n}x_{k}\|_{\text{\rm{dom}}(A)}=\|Q_{n}x_{k}\|+\|AQ_{n}x_{k}\|\longrightarrow 0\;\mbox{as}\;k\to\infty\;\mbox{for any}\;n\in{\mathbb{N}},

proving assertion (i). Assertion (ii) follows since
{\rm Graph}({\widetilde{A}})
is the closure in the
{\mathbb{Y}}\times{\mathbb{Y}}
topology of
{\rm Graph}(A)
. ∎

To discuss further properties of the linear operator
{\widetilde{A}}
we need the following real analysis result.

Remark 4.7
.

Let
({\mathscr{M}}_{1},d_{{\mathscr{M}}_{1}})
be a metric space,
({\mathscr{M}}_{2},d_{{\mathscr{M}}_{2}})
be a complete metric space,
{\mathscr{U}}_{1}\subseteq{\mathscr{M}}_{1}
,
f:{\mathscr{U}}_{1}\to{\mathscr{M}}_{2}
an uniformly continuous function. Then, there exists a unique uniformly continuous function
g:\overline{{\mathscr{U}}_{1}}\to{\mathscr{M}}_{2}
such that
g_{|{\mathscr{U}}_{1}}=f
. Here
\overline{{\mathscr{U}}_{1}}
denotes the closure of the set
{\mathscr{U}}_{1}
in the topology induced by the metric
d_{{\mathscr{M}}_{1}}
on
{\mathscr{M}}_{1}
.

Lemma 4.8
.

Assume Hypothesis (Q). Then, the following assertions hold true:

(i)

Q_{n}\big(\text{\rm{dom}}({\widetilde{A}})\big)\subseteq\text{\rm{dom}}(A)
for any
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
;

(ii)

Q_{n}AQ_{k}y=Q_{n}{\widetilde{A}}y
for any
y\in\text{\rm{dom}}({\widetilde{A}})
,
k,n\in{\mathbb{N}}
with
k\geq n+1
.

Proof.

(i) Fix
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
. First, we apply Remark
4.7
to
{\mathscr{M}}_{1}=\text{\rm{dom}}({\widetilde{A}})
endowed with the usual Frechet space metric,

(4.31)

d_{\widetilde{A}}:\text{\rm{dom}}({\widetilde{A}})\times\text{\rm{dom}}({\widetilde{A}})\to[0,\infty),\;\;d_{\widetilde{A}}(y_{1},y_{2})=\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{2^{j}}\frac{{\widetilde{\mathfrak{p}}}_{j}(y_{1}-y_{2})}{1+{\widetilde{\mathfrak{p}}}_{j}(y_{1}-y_{2})},

{\mathscr{M}}_{2}=\text{\rm{dom}}(A)
endowed with the usual Banach space metric induced by the norm
\|\cdot\|_{\text{\rm{dom}}(A)}
. We set
{\mathscr{U}}_{1}=\text{\rm{dom}}(A)
and
f=(Q_{n})_{|\text{\rm{dom}}(A)}
. From Lemma
4.6
(i) we have
f\in{\mathscr{C}}({\mathscr{U}}_{1},{\mathscr{M}}_{2})
and linear, hence,
f
is uniformly continuous. Since the closure of
{\mathscr{U}}_{1}
in the topology induced by the metric
d_{\widetilde{A}}
is equal to
\text{\rm{dom}}({\widetilde{A}})
, from Remark
4.7
we infer that there exists a unique uniformly continuous function
{\widetilde{Q}}_{n}:\text{\rm{dom}}({\widetilde{A}})\to\text{\rm{dom}}(A)
such that
({\widetilde{Q}}_{n})_{|\text{\rm{dom}}(A)}=(Q_{n})_{|\text{\rm{dom}}(A)}
. Since the function
(Q_{n})_{|\text{\rm{dom}}(A)}
is linear, from the uniqueness of the function
{\widetilde{Q}}_{n}
, we obtain that
{\widetilde{Q}}_{n}\in{\mathscr{C}}{\mathscr{L}}(\text{\rm{dom}}({\widetilde{A}}),\text{\rm{dom}}(A))
with respect to the topology induced by the family of seminorms
\{{\widetilde{\mathfrak{p}}}_{n}\}_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
on
\text{\rm{dom}}({\widetilde{A}})
and the graph norm
\|\cdot\|_{\text{\rm{dom}}(A)}
on
\text{\rm{dom}}(A)
.

Next, we note that since
\text{\rm{dom}}({\widetilde{A}})\hookrightarrow{\mathbb{Y}}
and
Q_{n}\in{\mathscr{C}}{\mathscr{L}}({\mathbb{Y}},{\mathbb{X}})
by Lemma
4.2
(vi), we infer that
(Q_{n})_{|\text{\rm{dom}}({\widetilde{A}})}\in{\mathscr{C}}{\mathscr{L}}(\text{\rm{dom}}({\widetilde{A}}),{\mathbb{X}})
. Since
{\widetilde{Q}}_{n}\in{\mathscr{C}}{\mathscr{L}}(\text{\rm{dom}}({\widetilde{A}}),\text{\rm{dom}}(A))
and
\text{\rm{dom}}(A)\hookrightarrow{\mathbb{X}}
we obtain

{\widetilde{Q}}_{n}\in{\mathscr{C}}{\mathscr{L}}(\text{\rm{dom}}({\widetilde{A}}),{\mathbb{X}})
. Moreover, we have

(4.32)

{\widetilde{Q}}_{n}x=(Q_{n})_{|\text{\rm{dom}}({\widetilde{A}})}x=Q_{n}x\;\mbox{for any}\;x\in\text{\rm{dom}}(A).

Since
\text{\rm{dom}}(A)
is a dense subspace of
\text{\rm{dom}}({\widetilde{A}})
in the topology induced by the family of seminorms
\{{\widetilde{\mathfrak{p}}}_{n}\}_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
by Lemma
4.6
(ii), from (
4.32
) we conclude that
{\widetilde{Q}}_{n}=(Q_{n})_{|\text{\rm{dom}}({\widetilde{A}})}
. It follows that

(4.33)

Q_{n}\big(\text{\rm{dom}}({\widetilde{A}})\big)={\widetilde{Q}}_{n}\big(\text{\rm{dom}}({\widetilde{A}})\big)\subseteq\text{\rm{dom}}(A).

Next, we fix
y\in\text{\rm{dom}}({\widetilde{A}})
. From (i), (
Q_{3}
) and (
Q_{6}
) we obtain

(4.34)

Q_{n}AQ_{m}y=Q_{n}AQ_{k}Q_{m}y=Q_{n}AQ_{k}y\;\mbox{for any}\;k,m,n\in{\mathbb{N}}\;\mbox{such that}\;m>k>n.

The sequence
\{Q_{n}AQ_{m}y\}_{m\in{\mathbb{N}}}
is constant for
m\geq n+1
for any
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
, which implies that the sequence
\{Q_{n}AQ_{m}y\}_{m\in{\mathbb{N}}}
is convergent in
{\mathbb{X}}
for any
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
. From (
Q_{8}
) we infer that the sequence
\{AQ_{m}y\}_{m\in{\mathbb{N}}}
is convergent in
{\mathbb{Y}}
. Let
z=\lim_{m\to\infty}AQ_{m}y
in
{\mathbb{Y}}
. Since
{\widetilde{A}}_{|\text{\rm{dom}}(A)}=A
from Lemma
4.2
(v) we have
Q_{m}y\in\text{\rm{dom}}(A)\subset\text{\rm{dom}}({\widetilde{A}})
for any
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
and

(4.35)

Q_{m}y\xrightarrow{\hskip 4.09723pt{\mathbb{Y}}\hskip 4.09723pt}y\;\mbox{and}\;{\widetilde{A}}Q_{m}y=AQ_{m}y\xrightarrow{\hskip 4.09723pt{\mathbb{Y}}\hskip 4.09723pt}z\;\mbox{as}\;m\to\infty.

Since the linear operator
{\widetilde{A}}
is closed we conclude that
z={\widetilde{A}}y
, which shows that
AQ_{m}y\xrightarrow{\hskip 4.09723pt{\mathbb{Y}}\hskip 4.09723pt}{\widetilde{A}}y
as
m\to\infty
. Assertion (ii) follows by passing to the limit as
m\to\infty
in (
4.34
).
∎

5.
Meromorphic extensions of
R_{A}(\cdot)_{|{\mathbb{X}}_{\infty}}
and integral representations of the Cosine and Sine functions

Obviously we cannot hope to have analytic (or meromorphic) extensions of the operator valued function
R_{A}(\cdot)
to a subset of the complex plane that contains the set
\Omega_{0}
defined in (
3.2
) and some part of the essential spectrum of the generator
A
. As showed in
[
15
]
for the case of Schrödinger operators, the best that we can hope for is to find meromorphic extensions of the operator-valued function
R_{A}(\cdot)_{|{\mathbb{X}}_{\infty}}
with values in the space
{\mathscr{C}}{\mathscr{L}}({\mathbb{X}}_{\infty},{\mathbb{Y}})
, where
{\mathbb{X}}_{\infty}
is the subspace of
{\mathbb{X}}
defined in (
4.5
). Therefore, we need to discuss some of the elementary properties of the subspace.

Remark 5.1
.

Assume Hypotheses (H) and (Q). Then, the following assertions hold true:

(i)

B\in{\mathscr{C}}{\mathscr{L}}({\mathbb{X}}_{\infty},{\mathbb{Y}})
if and only if
Q_{n}B\in\mathscr{B}({\mathbb{X}}_{\infty},{\mathbb{X}})
for any
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
;

(ii)

The space
{\mathscr{C}}{\mathscr{L}}({\mathbb{X}}_{\infty},{\mathbb{Y}})
is a Frechet space when endowed with the family of seminorms
\{{\widetilde{\mathfrak{q}}}_{n}\}_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
defined by

(5.1)

{\widetilde{\mathfrak{q}}}_{n}:{\mathscr{C}}{\mathscr{L}}({\mathbb{X}}_{\infty},{\mathbb{Y}})\to[0,\infty),\;{\widetilde{\mathfrak{q}}}_{n}(B)=\|Q_{n}B\|_{\mathscr{B}({\mathbb{X}})},n\in{\mathbb{N}};

(iii)

If
\Omega\subseteq{\mathbb{C}}
is an open connected set,
F:\Omega\to{\mathscr{C}}{\mathscr{L}}({\mathbb{X}}_{\infty},{\mathbb{Y}})
is an analytic function, then
Q_{n}F(\cdot)
is a
\mathscr{B}({\mathbb{X}}_{\infty},{\mathbb{X}})
-valued analytic function and
Q_{n}F(\cdot){Q_{j}}_{|{\mathbb{X}}}
is a
\mathscr{B}({\mathbb{X}})
-valued analytic function for any
n,j\in{\mathbb{N}}
;

(iv)

The operator-valued function

(5.2)

\lambda\to R_{A}(\lambda)_{|{\mathbb{X}}_{\infty}}:\Omega_{0}\setminus{\mathscr{M}}\to{\mathscr{C}}{\mathscr{L}}({\mathbb{X}}_{\infty},{\mathbb{Y}})\;\mbox{is analytic}.

We also recall the definition of a
finitely meromorphic
point given in Definition
3.1
for the case of Banach spaces. This definition can be extended naturally to the case of
{\mathscr{C}}{\mathscr{L}}({\mathbb{X}}_{\infty},{\mathbb{Y}})
-valued functions. Let
g_{0}
be the function from Hypothesis (H) above. Throughout this section we assume the following.

Hypothesis (H-ext).
We assume that there exist a number
\delta_{0}>0
, a function
g_{*}:{\mathbb{R}}\to{\mathbb{R}}
,
piecewise
of class
\mathscr{C}^{1}
, such that
g_{*}(s)+\delta_{0}\leq g_{0}(s)
for any
s\in{\mathbb{R}}
and such that

(i)

The function
R_{A}(\cdot)_{|{\mathbb{X}}_{\infty}}
with values in
{\mathscr{C}}{\mathscr{L}}({\mathbb{X}}_{\infty},{\mathbb{Y}})
has a meromorphic extension from
\Omega_{0}\setminus{\mathscr{M}}
to the set

(5.3)

\Omega_{*}=\{\lambda\in{\mathbb{C}}:{\mathrm{Re}}\lambda>g_{*}({\mathrm{Im}}\lambda)\}.

The extension will be denoted
R_{A}^{\infty}(\cdot)
;

(ii)

The operator-valued function
R_{A}^{\infty}(\cdot)
has finitely many singularities in
\Omega_{*}\setminus\Omega_{0}
, denoted
\nu_{\ell}
,
\ell=1,\dots,p
. All singularities are finitely meromorphic points of
R_{A}^{\infty}(\cdot)
;

(iii)

For any
i,j\in{\mathbb{N}}
there exist two piecewise continuous functions
{\widetilde{h}}_{i,j,1},{\widetilde{h}}_{i,j,2}:[0,\infty)\to[0,\infty)
such that

(5.4)

\|Q_{i}R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)Q_{j}\|\leq{\widetilde{h}}_{i,j,1}(|\lambda|){\widetilde{h}}_{i,j,2}({\mathrm{Re}}\lambda)\;\mbox{for any}\;\lambda\in\Omega_{*}\setminus\Omega_{0}\;\mbox{such that}\;|{\mathrm{Im}}\lambda|>\max_{1\leq\ell\leq p}|{\mathrm{Im}}\nu_{\ell}|.

(iv)

There exists
n_{*}\in{\mathbb{N}}
such that

(5.5)

\lim\limits_{s\to\infty}\frac{{\widetilde{h}}_{i,j,1}(s)}{s^{2n_{*}-1}}=0.

Moreover,
\sup\limits_{s\leq\omega}e^{\beta_{i,j}s}{\widetilde{h}}_{i,j,2}(s)<\infty
for some
\beta_{i,j}>0
, for any
i,j\in{\mathbb{N}}
.

In the sequel we denote the set of singularities by

(5.6)

{\mathscr{N}}=\{\nu_{\ell}:\ell=1,\dots,p\}.

Figure 3. A generic depiction of the sets introduced in Hypothesis (H-ext). The set
\Omega_{*}
consists of the union of the regions to the right of the graph of the curve
{\mathrm{Re}}\lambda=g_{*}({\mathrm{Im}}\lambda)
.

Our next task is to describe the immediate consequences of Hypothesis (H-ext), similar to Lemma
3.2
of Section
3
.

Lemma 5.2
.

Assume Hypotheses (H), (Q), (H-ext). Then, the following assertions hold true:

(i)

R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)Ax=\lambda^{2}R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)x-x
for any
\lambda\in\Omega_{*}\setminus\big({\mathscr{M}}\cup{\mathscr{N}}\big)
,
x\in\text{\rm{dom}}(A)\cap{\mathbb{X}}_{\infty}
;

(ii)

{\rm Range}\big(R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)\big)\subseteq\text{\rm{dom}}({\widetilde{A}})
and
{\widetilde{A}}R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)x=\lambda^{2}R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)x-x
for any
\lambda\in\Omega_{*}\setminus\big({\mathscr{M}}\cup{\mathscr{N}}\big)
,
x\in{\mathbb{X}}_{\infty}
;

(iii)

{\mathrm{Ker}}(Q_{i}-I_{\mathbb{Y}})
and
{\mathbb{X}}_{\infty}
are invariant subspaces of
A^{n}
for any
n,i\in{\mathbb{N}}
;

(iv)

For any
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
x\in\text{\rm{dom}}(A^{n})\cap{\mathbb{X}}_{\infty}
and
\lambda\in\Omega_{*}\setminus\big({\mathscr{M}}\cup{\mathscr{N}}\big)
we have

(5.7)

R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)A^{n}x=\lambda^{2n}R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)x-\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}\lambda^{2n-2-2j}A^{j}x.

Proof.

Fix
x\in\text{\rm{dom}}(A)\cap{\mathbb{X}}_{\infty}
. From Lemma
3.2
(i)-(ii) we infer that

(5.8)

R_{A}(\lambda)Ax=\lambda^{2}R_{A}(\lambda)x-x\;\mbox{for any}\;\lambda\in\Omega_{0}\setminus{\mathscr{M}}.

Assertion (i) follows from (
5.8
) because
R_{A}^{\infty}(\cdot)
is the analytic extension of
R_{A}(\cdot)_{|{\mathbb{X}}_{\infty}}
to
\Omega_{*}\setminus\big({\mathscr{M}}\cup{\mathscr{N}}\big)
by Hypothesis (H-ext).

Next, we fix
x\in{\mathbb{X}}_{\infty}
and define the function
{\widetilde{F}}_{x}:\Omega_{*}\setminus\big({\mathscr{M}}\cup{\mathscr{N}}\big)\to{\mathbb{Y}}\times{\mathbb{Y}}

by
{\widetilde{F}}_{x}(\lambda)=(R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)x,\lambda^{2}R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)x-x)
. By Hypothesis (H-ext)
{\widetilde{F}}_{x}
is analytic. From Lemma
3.2
(i)-(ii) we obtain

(5.9)

{\widetilde{F}}_{x}(\lambda)=\bigl(R_{A}(\lambda)x,\lambda^{2}R_{A}(\lambda)x-x\bigr)\in\mathrm{Graph}(A)\subseteq{\rm Graph}({\widetilde{A}})

for any
\lambda\in\Omega_{0}\setminus{\mathscr{M}}
. Since the linear operator
{\widetilde{A}}
is closed,
{\rm Graph}({\widetilde{A}})
is a closed subspace of the Frechet space
{\mathbb{Y}}\times{\mathbb{Y}}
. From Lemma
B.3
and (
5.9
) we derive that
{\widetilde{F}}_{x}(\lambda)\in{\rm Graph}({\widetilde{A}})
for any
\lambda\in\Omega_{*}\setminus\big({\mathscr{M}}\cup{\mathscr{N}}\big)
, proving assertion
(ii).

Using induction and Hypothesis (
Q_{5}
) we obtain

(5.10)

A^{n}\big(\text{\rm{dom}}(A^{n})\cap{\mathrm{Ker}}(Q_{i}-I_{\mathbb{Y}})\big)\subseteq{\mathrm{Ker}}(Q_{i}-I_{\mathbb{Y}})\;\mbox{for any}\;n,i\in{\mathbb{N}},

which implies the subspace
{\mathbb{X}}_{\infty}
is invariant under
A^{n}
, that is

(5.11)

A^{n}\big(\text{\rm{dom}}(A^{n})\cap{\mathbb{X}}_{\infty})\big)\subseteq{\mathbb{X}}_{\infty}\;\mbox{for any}\;n\in{\mathbb{N}}.

Assertion (iv) follows from (i) by induction, similar to the proof of Lemma
3.2
(iii).
∎

Lemma 5.3
.

Assume Hypotheses (H), (Q), (H-ext). Then, the following assertions hold true:

(i)

\nu_{\ell}^{2}\in\sigma_{\mathrm{point}}({\widetilde{A}})
for any
\ell=1,\dots,p
;

(ii)

There exists
{\widetilde{r}}_{*}>0
, small enough, such that

(5.12)

{\widetilde{P}}_{\ell}=\frac{1}{\pi{\mathrm{i}}}\int_{\partial D(\nu_{\ell},{\widetilde{r}}_{*})}\lambda R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda){\mathrm{d}}\lambda\in{\mathscr{C}}{\mathscr{L}}({\mathbb{X}}_{\infty},{\mathbb{Y}}),\;{\widehat{P}}_{\ell}=\frac{1}{\pi{\mathrm{i}}}\int_{\partial D(\nu_{\ell},{\widetilde{r}}_{*})}R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda){\mathrm{d}}\lambda\in{\mathscr{C}}{\mathscr{L}}({\mathbb{X}}_{\infty},{\mathbb{Y}})

are finite rank operators for any
\ell=1,\dots,p
;

(iii)

{\widetilde{P}}_{\ell}\big(\text{\rm{dom}}(A)\cap{\mathbb{X}}_{\infty}\big)={\rm Range}({\widetilde{P}}_{\ell})\subset\text{\rm{dom}}({\widetilde{A}})
,
{\widehat{P}}_{\ell}\big(\text{\rm{dom}}(A)\cap{\mathbb{X}}_{\infty}\big)={\rm Range}({\widehat{P}}_{\ell})\subset\text{\rm{dom}}({\widetilde{A}})
and

{\widetilde{A}}{\widetilde{P}}_{\ell}x={\widetilde{P}}_{\ell}Ax
,
{\widetilde{A}}{\widehat{P}}_{\ell}x={\widehat{P}}_{\ell}Ax
for any
x\in\text{\rm{dom}}(A)\cap{\mathbb{X}}_{\infty}
,
\ell=1,\dots,p
. Moreover,
{\rm Range}({\widetilde{P}}_{\ell})
and
{\rm Range}({\widehat{P}}_{\ell})
are invariant under
{\widetilde{A}}
for any
\ell=1,\dots,p
;

(iv)

For any
\ell\in\{1,\dots,p\}
there exists
{\widetilde{N}}_{\ell}\in{\mathbb{N}}
such that
{\rm Range}({\widetilde{P}}_{\ell})\subset\text{\rm{dom}}({\widetilde{A}}^{{\widetilde{N}}_{\ell}})
and
({\widetilde{A}}-\nu_{\ell}^{2}I_{\mathbb{Y}})_{|\mathrm{Range}({\widetilde{P}}_{\ell})}
is nilpotent;

(v)

If
\nu_{\ell}=0
then there exists
{\widetilde{N}}_{\ell}\in{\mathbb{N}}
such that
{\rm Range}({\widehat{P}}_{\ell})\subset\text{\rm{dom}}({\widetilde{A}}^{{\widetilde{N}}_{\ell}})
and
{\widetilde{A}}_{|\mathrm{Range}({\widehat{P}}_{\ell})}
is nilpotent;

(vi)

If
\nu_{\ell}\neq 0
then there exists an analytic function
{\widetilde{E}}_{\ell}:D(\nu_{\ell},2{\widetilde{r}}_{*})\to{\mathscr{C}}{\mathscr{L}}({\mathbb{X}}_{\infty},{\mathbb{Y}})
such that

(5.13)

R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)=\sum_{n=1}^{{\widetilde{N}}_{\ell}}\frac{1}{(\lambda^{2}-\nu_{\ell}^{2})^{n}}({\widetilde{A}}-\nu_{\ell}^{2}I_{\mathbb{Y}})^{n-1}{\widetilde{P}}_{\ell}+{\widetilde{E}}_{\ell}(\lambda)\;\mbox{for any}\;\lambda\in D(\nu_{\ell},2{\widetilde{r}}_{*})\setminus\{\nu_{\ell}\};

(vii)

If
\nu_{\ell}=0
then there exists
{\widehat{N}}_{\ell}\in{\mathbb{N}}
with
{\widehat{N}}_{\ell}\leq{\widetilde{N}}_{\ell}
and an analytic function
{\widetilde{E}}_{\ell}:D(0,2{\widetilde{r}}_{*})\to{\mathscr{C}}{\mathscr{L}}({\mathbb{X}}_{\infty},{\mathbb{Y}})
such that

(5.14)

R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)=\sum_{j=1}^{{\widehat{N}}_{\ell}}\frac{1}{\lambda^{2j}}{\widetilde{A}}^{j-1}{\widetilde{P}}_{\ell}+\sum_{j=1}^{{\widehat{N}}_{\ell}}\frac{1}{\lambda^{2j+1}}{\widetilde{A}}^{j-1}{\widehat{P}}_{\ell}+{\widetilde{E}}_{\ell}(\lambda)\;\mbox{for any}\;\lambda\in D(0,2{\widetilde{r}}_{*})\setminus\{0\};

(viii)

If
\nu_{\ell}\neq 0
then

(5.15)

{\mathrm{Res}}\Bigl(\lambda^{\kappa}e^{\lambda t}R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda),\lambda=\nu_{\ell}\Bigr)=\sum_{j=1}^{{\widetilde{N}}_{\ell}}\Bigg(\frac{1}{2\pi{\mathrm{i}}}\int_{\partial D(\nu_{\ell},{\widetilde{r}}_{*})}\frac{\lambda^{\kappa}e^{\lambda t}}{(\lambda^{2}-\nu_{k}^{2})^{j}}{\mathrm{d}}\lambda\Bigg)({\widetilde{A}}-\nu_{\ell}^{2}I_{\mathbb{Y}})^{j-1}{\widetilde{P}}_{\ell},\;\kappa=0,1;

(ix)

If
\nu_{\ell}=0
then

(5.16)

{\mathrm{Res}}\Bigl(\lambda^{\kappa}e^{\lambda t}R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda),\lambda=0\Bigr)=\sum_{j=1}^{{\widehat{N}}_{\ell}}\frac{t^{2j-1-\kappa}}{(2j-1-\kappa)!}{\widetilde{A}}^{j-1}{\widetilde{P}}_{\ell}+\sum_{j=1}^{{\widehat{N}}_{\ell}}\frac{t^{2j-\kappa}}{(2j-\kappa)!}{\widetilde{A}}^{j-1}{\widehat{P}}_{\ell},\;\kappa=0,1.

Proof.

Let
{\widetilde{r}}_{*}>0
be a positive number independent of
\ell\in\{1,\dots,p\}
such that the elements of the set
{\mathscr{N}}_{\pm}:=\{\nu_{\ell}:\ell=1,\dots,p\}\cup\{-\nu_{\ell}:\ell=1,\dots,p\}
are separated by disks of radius
2{\widetilde{r}}_{*}
, that is

(5.17)

D(\xi,2{\widetilde{r}}_{*})\cap{\mathscr{N}}_{\pm}=\{\xi\}\;\mbox{for any}\;\xi\in{\mathscr{N}}_{\pm}.

Fix
\ell\in\{1,\dots,p\}
. Since
\nu_{l}
is a finitely meromorphic point by Hypothesis (H-ext) (ii), by Definition
3.1
there exists
{\widetilde{N}}_{\ell}\in{\mathbb{N}}
, an analytic operator-valued function
{\overline{E}}_{\ell}:D(\nu_{\ell},2{\widetilde{r}}_{*})\to{\mathscr{C}}{\mathscr{L}}({\mathbb{X}}_{\infty},{\mathbb{Y}})
and finite rank operators
{\widetilde{R}}_{\ell,j}\in{\mathscr{C}}{\mathscr{L}}({\mathbb{X}}_{\infty},{\mathbb{Y}})
,
j=1,\dots,{\widetilde{N}}_{\ell}
, such that

(5.18)

R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)=\sum_{j=1}^{{\widetilde{N}}_{\ell}}\frac{1}{(\lambda-\nu_{\ell})^{j}}{\widetilde{R}}_{\ell,j}+{\overline{E}}_{\ell}(\lambda)\;\mbox{for any}\;\lambda\in D(\nu_{\ell},2{\widetilde{r}}_{*})\setminus\{\nu_{\ell}\}.

Integrating, one can readily check that

(5.19)

{\widetilde{R}}_{\ell,j}=\frac{1}{2\pi{\mathrm{i}}}\int_{\partial D(\nu_{\ell},{\widetilde{r}}_{*})}(\lambda-\nu_{l})^{j-1}R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda){\mathrm{d}}\lambda\;\mbox{for any}\;j=1,\dots,{\widetilde{N}}_{\ell}.

Next, we define

(5.20)

{\widetilde{G}}_{\ell,n}=\frac{1}{\pi{\mathrm{i}}}\int_{\partial D(\nu_{\ell},{\widetilde{r}}_{*})}\lambda(\lambda^{2}-\nu_{\ell}^{2})^{n-1}R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda){\mathrm{d}}\lambda\in{\mathscr{C}}{\mathscr{L}}({\mathbb{X}}_{\infty},{\mathbb{Y}})\;\mbox{for}\;n\in{\mathbb{N}}.

From (
5.18
) and (
5.20
) we obtain that

(5.21)

{\widetilde{G}}_{\ell,n}=\frac{1}{\pi{\mathrm{i}}}\sum_{j=1}^{{\widetilde{N}}_{\ell}}\Bigg(\int_{\partial D(\nu_{\ell},{\widetilde{r}}_{*})}\frac{\lambda\bigl(\lambda^{2}-\nu_{\ell}^{2}\bigr)^{n-1}}{(\lambda-\nu_{\ell})^{j}}{\mathrm{d}}\lambda\Bigg){\widetilde{R}}_{\ell,j}\;\mbox{for any}\;n\in{\mathbb{N}}.

Since
{\widetilde{R}}_{\ell,j}
is a finite rank operator for any
j=1,\dots,{\widetilde{N}}_{\ell}
, from (
5.21
) we conclude that

(5.22)

{\widetilde{G}}_{\ell,n}=0\;\mbox{for any}\;n\geq 1+{\widetilde{N}}_{\ell},\;\;{\widetilde{G}}_{\ell,n}\;\mbox{is of finite rank for any}\;n=1,\dots,{\widetilde{N}}_{\ell}.

From Lemma
5.2
(ii) and
[
2
, Proposition 1.1.7]
it follows that
{\rm Range}({\widetilde{G}}_{\ell,n})\subseteq\text{\rm{dom}}({\widetilde{A}})
and

\displaystyle({\widetilde{A}}-\nu_{\ell}^{2}I_{\mathbb{Y}}){\widetilde{G}}_{\ell,n}x

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\pi{\mathrm{i}}}\int_{\partial D(\nu_{\ell},{\widetilde{r}}_{*})}\lambda\bigl(\lambda^{2}-\nu_{\ell}^{2}\bigr)^{n-1}({\widetilde{A}}-\nu_{\ell}^{2}I_{\mathbb{Y}})R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)x{\mathrm{d}}\lambda

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\pi{\mathrm{i}}}\int_{\partial D(\nu_{\ell},{\widetilde{r}}_{*})}\lambda\bigl(\lambda^{2}-\nu_{\ell}^{2}\bigr)^{n-1}\Bigl((\lambda^{2}-\nu_{\ell}^{2})R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)x-x\Bigr){\mathrm{d}}\lambda

(5.23)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\pi{\mathrm{i}}}\int_{\partial D(\nu_{\ell},{\widetilde{r}}_{*})}\lambda\bigl(\lambda^{2}-\nu_{\ell}^{2}\bigr)^{n}R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)x{\mathrm{d}}\lambda={\widetilde{G}}_{\ell,n+1}x

for any
x\in{\mathbb{X}}_{\infty}
,
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
. From (
5.22
) we conclude that
{\widetilde{P}}_{\ell}={\widetilde{G}}_{\ell,1}
and
{\widehat{P}}_{\ell}={\widetilde{R}}_{\ell,1}
are finite rank operators for any
\ell=1,\dots,p
, proving assertion (ii). Moreover, from (
5
) we have

(5.24)

{\rm Range}({\widetilde{P}}_{\ell})\subset\text{\rm{dom}}({\widetilde{A}}^{{\widetilde{N}}_{\ell}})\;\mbox{and}\;{\widetilde{G}}_{\ell,n}=({\widetilde{A}}-\nu_{\ell}^{2}I_{\mathbb{Y}})^{n-1}{\widetilde{P}}_{\ell}\;\mbox{for any}\;\ell=1,\dots,p,\,n=1,\dots,{\widetilde{N}}_{\ell}+1.

From Lemma
5.2
and
[
2
, Proposition 1.1.7]
we obtain
{\rm Range}({\widehat{P}}_{\ell})\subseteq\text{\rm{dom}}({\widetilde{A}})
and

\displaystyle{\widetilde{A}}{\widetilde{P}}_{\ell}x=\frac{1}{\pi{\mathrm{i}}}\int_{\partial D(\nu_{\ell},{\widetilde{r}}_{*})}\lambda{\widetilde{A}}R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)x{\mathrm{d}}\lambda=\frac{1}{\pi{\mathrm{i}}}\int_{\partial D(\nu_{\ell},{\widetilde{r}}_{*})}\lambda R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)Ax{\mathrm{d}}\lambda={\widetilde{P}}_{\ell}Ax,

(5.25)

\displaystyle{\widetilde{A}}{\widehat{P}}_{\ell}x=\frac{1}{2\pi{\mathrm{i}}}\int_{\partial D(\nu_{\ell},{\widetilde{r}}_{*})}{\widetilde{A}}R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)x{\mathrm{d}}\lambda=\frac{1}{2\pi{\mathrm{i}}}\int_{\partial D(\nu_{\ell},{\widetilde{r}}_{*})}R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)Ax{\mathrm{d}}\lambda={\widehat{P}}_{\ell}Ax

for any
x\in\text{\rm{dom}}(A)\cap{\mathbb{X}}_{\infty}
. From Lemma
4.2
(ix) and since
{\widetilde{P}}_{\ell},{\widehat{P}}_{\ell}\in{\mathscr{C}}{\mathscr{L}}({\mathbb{X}}_{\infty},{\mathbb{Y}})
we infer that
{\widetilde{P}}_{\ell}(\text{\rm{dom}}(A)\cap{\mathbb{X}}_{\infty})
and
{\widehat{P}}_{\ell}(\text{\rm{dom}}(A)\cap{\mathbb{X}}_{\infty})
are dense subspaces of
{\rm Range}({\widetilde{P}}_{\ell})
and
{\rm Range}({\widehat{P}}_{\ell})
, respectively. Since
{\rm Range}({\widetilde{P}}_{\ell})
and
{\rm Range}({\widehat{P}}_{\ell})
are finite dimensional vector spaces, we infer that
{\widetilde{P}}_{\ell}\big(\text{\rm{dom}}(A)\cap{\mathbb{X}}_{\infty}\big)={\rm Range}({\widetilde{P}}_{\ell})
,
