{\widehat{P}}_{\ell}\big(\text{\rm{dom}}(A)\cap{\mathbb{X}}_{\infty}\big)={\rm Range}({\widehat{P}}_{\ell})
. This fact together with (
5
) proves assertion (iii).
From (
5.22
) and (
5.24
) we obtain
({\widetilde{A}}-\nu_{\ell}^{2}I_{\mathbb{Y}})^{{\widetilde{N}}_{\ell}}{\widetilde{P}}_{\ell}=0
. In addition, since
\nu_{\ell}
is a singularity of
R_{A}^{\infty}(\cdot)
by Hypothesis (H-ext) it follows that
{\widetilde{P}}_{\ell}\neq 0
for any
\ell=1,\dots,p
.
From (iii) we conclude that
({\widetilde{A}}-\nu_{\ell}^{2}I_{\mathbb{Y}})_{|{\rm Range}({\widetilde{P}}_{\ell})}
is a nilpotent linear operator on the non-trivial, finite dimensional space
{\rm Range}({\widetilde{P}}_{\ell})
, proving assertion (iv). Assertion (i) follows immediately from assertion (iv) and since
{\widetilde{P}}_{\ell}\neq 0
.

To prove the remaining assertions of the lemma we distinguish between the case when the resonance
\nu_{\ell}
is at a non-zero value or at zero.

Case 1.

\nu_{\ell}\neq 0
.

We fix
\lambda\in D(\nu_{\ell},2{\widetilde{r}}_{*})\setminus\{\nu_{\ell}\}
and
r\in\Big(0,\min\big\{{\widetilde{r}}_{*},\frac{|\lambda^{2}-\nu_{\ell}^{2}|}{4({\overline{\nu}}+{\widetilde{r}}_{*})}\big\}\Big)
, where
{\overline{\nu}}=\max\limits_{1\leq\ell\leq p}|\nu_{\ell}|
. From (
5.21
) and (
5.22
) we have

\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{{\widetilde{N}}_{\ell}}\frac{1}{(\lambda^{2}-\nu_{\ell}^{2})^{n}}{\widetilde{G}}_{\ell,n}

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{(\lambda^{2}-\nu_{\ell}^{2})^{n}}{\widetilde{G}}_{\ell,n}=\frac{1}{\pi{\mathrm{i}}}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{j=1}^{{\widetilde{N}}_{\ell}}\Bigg(\int_{\partial D(\nu_{\ell},r)}\frac{\xi\bigl(\xi^{2}-\nu_{\ell}^{2}\bigr)^{n-1}}{(\lambda^{2}-\nu_{\ell}^{2})^{n}(\xi-\nu_{\ell})^{j}}{\mathrm{d}}\xi\Bigg){\widetilde{R}}_{\ell,j}

(5.26)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\pi{\mathrm{i}}}\sum_{j=1}^{{\widetilde{N}}_{\ell}}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\Bigg(\int_{\partial D(\nu_{\ell},r)}\frac{\xi\bigl(\xi^{2}-\nu_{\ell}^{2}\bigr)^{n-1}}{(\lambda^{2}-\nu_{\ell}^{2})^{n}(\xi-\nu_{\ell})^{j}}{\mathrm{d}}\xi\Bigg){\widetilde{R}}_{\ell,j}.

An elementary computation shows that

(5.27)

\int_{\partial D(\nu,r)}\frac{d\xi}{(\xi-\nu)^{k}(\xi-\mu)}=2\pi{\mathrm{i}}\frac{(-1)^{k-1}}{(\nu-\mu)}\;\mbox{whenever}\;k\in{\mathbb{N}},|\nu-\mu|>r.

Moreover, we note that

(5.28)

\Bigg|\frac{\xi^{2}-\nu_{\ell}^{2}}{\lambda^{2}-\nu_{\ell}^{2}}\Bigg|=\frac{(|\xi-\nu_{\ell}|)(|\xi|+|\nu_{\ell}|)}{|\lambda^{2}-\nu_{\ell}^{2}|}\leq\frac{r(2{\overline{\nu}}+{\widetilde{r}}_{*})}{|\lambda^{2}-\nu_{\ell}^{2}|}\leq\frac{1}{2}.

From (
5.27
) and (
5.28
) we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}

\displaystyle\Bigg(\int_{\partial D(\nu_{\ell},r)}\frac{\xi\bigl(\xi^{2}-\nu_{\ell}^{2}\bigr)^{n-1}{\mathrm{d}}\xi}{(\lambda^{2}-\nu_{\ell}^{2})^{n}(\xi-\nu_{\ell})^{j}}\Bigg)=\int_{\partial D(\nu_{\ell},r)}\frac{\xi}{(\xi-\nu_{\ell})^{j}(\lambda^{2}-\xi^{2})}{\mathrm{d}}\xi

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\partial D(\nu_{\ell},r)}\frac{{\mathrm{d}}\xi}{(\xi-\nu_{\ell})^{j}(\xi-\lambda)}-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\partial D(\nu_{\ell},r)}\frac{{\mathrm{d}}\xi}{(\xi-\nu_{\ell})^{j}(\xi+\lambda)}

(5.29)

\displaystyle=\frac{\pi{\mathrm{i}}}{(\lambda-\nu_{\ell})^{j}}+\frac{(-1)^{j}\pi{\mathrm{i}}}{(\lambda+\nu_{\ell})^{j}}\;\mbox{for any}\;j=1,\dots,{\widetilde{N}}_{\ell}.

From (
5
) and (
5
) we conclude that

(5.30)

\sum_{j=1}^{{\widetilde{N}}_{\ell}}\frac{1}{(\lambda-\nu_{\ell})^{j}}{\widetilde{R}}_{\ell,j}=\sum_{n=1}^{{\widetilde{N}}_{\ell}}\frac{1}{(\lambda^{2}-\nu_{\ell}^{2})^{n}}{\widetilde{G}}_{\ell,n}+\sum_{j=1}^{{\widetilde{N}}_{\ell}}\frac{(-1)^{j-1}}{(\lambda+\nu_{\ell})^{j}}{\widetilde{R}}_{\ell,j}\;\mbox{for any}\;\lambda\in D(\nu_{\ell},2{\widetilde{r}}_{*})\setminus\{\nu_{\ell}\}.

From (
5.18
), (
5.24
) and (
5.30
) we infer that the Laurent expansion (
5.13
) holds, with
{\widetilde{E}}_{\ell}:D(\nu_{\ell},2{\widetilde{r}}_{*})\to{\mathscr{C}}{\mathscr{L}}({\mathbb{X}}_{\infty},{\mathbb{Y}})
defined by

(5.31)

{\widetilde{E}}_{\ell}(\lambda)={\overline{E}}_{\ell}(\lambda)+\sum_{j=1}^{{\widetilde{N}}_{\ell}}\frac{(-1)^{j-1}}{(\lambda+\nu_{\ell})^{j}}{\widetilde{R}}_{\ell,j}.

Since
\nu_{\ell}\neq 0
by (
5.17
) we have
-\nu_{\ell}\notin D(\nu_{\ell},2{\widetilde{r}}_{*})
, which implies that
{\widetilde{E}}_{\ell}
is analytic, proving assertion (vi). Assertion (viii) follows immediately from assertion (vi).

Case 2.

\nu_{\ell}=0
.

First, we set
{\widetilde{R}}_{\ell,j}=0
for each
j\geq{\widetilde{N}}_{\ell}+1
. Using again Lemma
5.2
(ii) and
[
2
, Proposition 1.1.7]
we obtain that
{\rm Range}({\widetilde{R}}_{\ell,j})\subseteq\text{\rm{dom}}({\widetilde{A}})
and

\displaystyle{\widetilde{A}}{\widetilde{R}}_{\ell,j}x

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi{\mathrm{i}}}\int_{\partial D(0,{\widetilde{r}}_{*})}\lambda^{j-1}{\widetilde{A}}R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)x{\mathrm{d}}\lambda=\frac{1}{2\pi{\mathrm{i}}}\int_{\partial D(0,{\widetilde{r}}_{*})}\lambda^{j-1}\Bigl(\lambda^{2}R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)x-x\Bigr){\mathrm{d}}\lambda

(5.32)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi{\mathrm{i}}}\int_{\partial D(0,{\widetilde{r}}_{*})}\lambda^{j+1}R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)x{\mathrm{d}}\lambda={\widetilde{R}}_{\ell,j+2}x\;\mbox{for any}\;x\in{\mathbb{X}}_{\infty},\,j\in{\mathbb{N}}.

Since
{\widehat{P}}_{\ell}={\widetilde{R}}_{\ell,1}
it follows that
{\rm Range}({\widehat{P}}_{\ell})\subset\text{\rm{dom}}({\widetilde{A}}^{{\widetilde{N}}_{\ell}})
and

(5.33)

{\widetilde{R}}_{\ell,2j+1}={\widetilde{A}}^{j-1}{\widehat{P}}_{\ell}\;\mbox{and}\;{\widetilde{R}}_{\ell,2j}={\widetilde{A}}^{j-1}{\widetilde{P}}_{\ell}\;\mbox{for any}\;j\in{\mathbb{N}}.

It follows that
{\widetilde{A}}^{{\widetilde{N}}_{\ell}}{\widehat{P}}_{\ell}=0
, which implies that
{\widetilde{A}}_{|{\rm Range}({\widehat{P}}_{\ell})}
is nilpotent proving assertion (v). Next, we define
{\widehat{N}}_{\ell}=[{\widetilde{N}}_{\ell}/2]+1
, where
[\cdot]
denotes the integer part. Assertion (vii) follows from (
5.18
) and (
5.33
). Assertion (ix) follows immediately from assertion (vii).
∎

Remark 5.4
.

We note that the residue formula (
3.13
) with
\mu_{k}=0
has either even or odd terms in
t
for the case when
\kappa=0
or
\kappa=1
, respectively. Meanwhile, (
5.16
) always has even and odd terms in both cases. This difference is due to the fact that if
0
is a discrete eigenvalue of finite algebraic multiplicity, then in a neighborhood of
0
we have
R_{A}(\lambda)=R(\lambda^{2},A)
. In the case when
0
is a resonance, it is possible that the Laurent series representation of
R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)
has odd terms in
\lambda
, see, e.g.,
[
15
, Theorem 2.2.7]
.

Remark 5.5
.

As in Remark
3.4
, an elementary computation shows that for any
\kappa=0,1
,
\ell=1,\dots,p
,
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
there exists a polynomial
{\widetilde{p}}_{\kappa,\ell,k}
of degree at most
k-1
such that

(5.34)

\frac{1}{2\pi{\mathrm{i}}}\int_{\partial D(\nu_{k},{\widetilde{r}}_{*})}\frac{\lambda^{\kappa}e^{\lambda t}}{(\lambda^{2}-\nu_{\ell}^{2})^{k}}{\mathrm{d}}\lambda={\widetilde{p}}_{\kappa,\ell,k}(t)e^{\nu_{\ell}t}\;\mbox{for any}\;t\in{\mathbb{R}}.

Next, we discuss the properties of the residues of the operator-valued functions that involve
R_{A}^{\infty}
.

Lemma 5.6
.

Assume Hypotheses (H), (Q), (H-ext)
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
and
x\in\text{\rm{dom}}(A^{n})\cap{\mathbb{X}}_{\infty}
. Then,

(i)

{\mathrm{Res}}\Bigl(\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n-1}}R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)A^{n}x,\lambda=\nu_{\ell}\Bigr)={\mathrm{Res}}\Bigl(\lambda e^{\lambda t}R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)x,\lambda=\nu_{\ell}\Bigr)
, if
\nu_{\ell}\neq 0
;

(ii)

{\mathrm{Res}}\Bigl(\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n-1}}R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)A^{n}x,\lambda=0\Bigr)={\mathrm{Res}}\Bigl(\lambda e^{\lambda t}R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)x,\lambda=0\Bigr)-\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}\frac{t^{2j}}{(2j)!}A^{j}x
, if
\nu_{\ell}=0
;

(iii)

{\mathrm{Res}}\Bigl(\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n-1}}R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)A^{n}x,\lambda=0\Bigr)=-\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}\frac{t^{2j}}{(2j)!}A^{j}x
, if
0\in\Omega_{*}\setminus\big({\mathscr{M}}\cup{\mathscr{N}}\big)
.

Proof.

From Lemma
5.2
(iii) we have

(5.35)

\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n-1}}R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)A^{n}x=\lambda R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)x-\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2j+1}}A^{j}x

for any
\lambda\in\Omega\setminus\bigl({\mathscr{M}}\cup{\mathscr{N}}\cup\{0\}\bigr)
. Assertions (i)-(iii) follow from (
3.30
) and (
5.35
).
∎

Lemma 5.7
.

Assume Hypotheses (H), (Q), (H-ext),
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
x\in\text{\rm{dom}}(A^{n-1})\cap{\mathbb{X}}_{\infty}
. Then,

(i)

{\mathrm{Res}}\Bigl(\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n}}R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)A^{n-1}x,\lambda=\nu\Bigr)\in\text{\rm{dom}}({\widetilde{A}})
for any
\nu\in{\mathscr{N}}\cup\{0\}
;

(ii)

{\widetilde{A}}{\mathrm{Res}}\Bigl(\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n}}R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)A^{n-1}x,\lambda=\nu\Bigr)={\mathrm{Res}}\Bigl(e^{\lambda t}R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)x,\lambda=\nu\Bigr)
, if
\nu\in{\mathscr{N}}\setminus\{0\}
;

(iii)

{\widetilde{A}}{\mathrm{Res}}\Bigl(\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n}}R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)A^{n-1}x,\lambda=0\Bigr)={\mathrm{Res}}\Bigl(e^{\lambda t}R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)x,\lambda=0\Bigr)-\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}\frac{t^{2j+1}}{(2j+1)!}A^{j}x
, if
0\in{\mathscr{N}}
;

(iv)

{\widetilde{A}}{\mathrm{Res}}\Bigl(\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n}}R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)A^{n-1}x,\lambda=0\Bigr)=-\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}\frac{t^{2j+1}}{(2j+1)!}A^{j}x
, if
0\in\Omega_{*}\setminus\big({\mathscr{M}}\cup{\mathscr{N}}\big)
.

Proof.

From Lemma
5.2
(i),

(5.36)

\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n}}R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)A^{n-1}x\in\text{\rm{dom}}({\widetilde{A}})\;\mbox{and}\;{\widetilde{A}}\Bigl(\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n}}R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)A^{n-1}x\Bigr)=\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n-2}}R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)A^{n-1}x-\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n}}A^{n-1}x

for any
\lambda\in\Omega_{*}\setminus\bigl({\mathscr{M}}\cup{\mathscr{N}}\cup\{0\}\bigr)
. Assertion (i) follows from (
5.36
) and
[
2
, Proposition 1.1.7]
. Moreover, we have

(5.37)

{\widetilde{A}}{\mathrm{Res}}\Bigl(\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n}}R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)A^{n-1}x,\lambda=\nu\Bigr)={\mathrm{Res}}\Bigl(\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n-2}}R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)A^{n-1}x,\lambda=\nu\Bigr)-{\mathrm{Res}}\Bigl(\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n}},\lambda=\nu\Bigr)A^{n-1}x

for any
\nu\in{\mathscr{N}}\cup\{0\}
. From Lemma
5.2
(iii) it follows that

(5.38)

\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n-2}}R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)A^{n-1}x=e^{\lambda t}R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)x-\sigma_{n}(\lambda,t,x)

for any
\lambda\in\Omega_{*}\setminus\bigl({\mathscr{M}}\cup{\mathscr{N}}\cup\{0\}\bigr)
, where the function
\sigma_{n}:{\mathbb{C}}\setminus\{0\}\times{\mathbb{R}}\times\text{\rm{dom}}(A^{n-1})\to{\mathbb{X}}
was introduced in (
3.34
).
From (
3.34
), (
3.35
), (
5.37
) and (
5.38
) we obtain

(5.39)

{\widetilde{A}}{\mathrm{Res}}\Bigl(\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n}}R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)A^{n-1}x,\lambda=\nu\Bigr)={\mathrm{Res}}\Bigl(e^{\lambda t}R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)x,\lambda=\nu\Bigr)-\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}{\mathrm{Res}}\Bigl(\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2j+2}},\lambda=\nu\Bigr)A^{j}x

for any
\nu\in{\mathscr{N}}\cup\{0\}
. Assertions (ii)-(iv) follow from (
3.30
) and (
5.39
). ∎

Next, we aim to switch the integral in the representation formulas of the cosine family and its associated sine family given in Theorem
3.11
and Theorem
3.12
, to a path along the complex curve
{\mathrm{Re}}\lambda=g_{*}({\mathrm{Im}}\lambda)+\varepsilon
for some
\varepsilon>0
small enough. Similarly to Section
3
, see (
3.41
), we define

(5.40)

{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}_{0}:=\frac{1}{4}\min\bigl\{\varepsilon_{0},\delta_{0},{\mathrm{Re}}\nu_{1}-g_{*}({\mathrm{Im}}\nu_{1}),\dots,{\mathrm{Re}}\nu_{p}-g_{*}({\mathrm{Im}}\nu_{p})\bigr\}>0.

The positive constant
\delta_{0}
was introduced in Hypothesis (H-ext).
Similarly to (
3
), for any
a>\max_{1\leq\ell\leq p}|{\mathrm{Im}}\nu_{\ell}|
and
\varepsilon\in(0,{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}_{0})
we define the closed simple path
{\widetilde{\Gamma}}_{a}^{\varepsilon}
oriented counterclockwise, defined as the union
\Gamma_{a,{\mathrm{mid}}}^{\varepsilon}\cup{\widetilde{\Gamma}}_{a,{\mathrm{up}}}^{\varepsilon}\cup{\widetilde{\Gamma}}_{a,{\mathrm{down}}}^{\varepsilon}\cup-{\widetilde{\Gamma}}_{a,{\mathrm{mid}}}^{\varepsilon}
, where

\displaystyle{\widetilde{\Gamma}}_{a,{\mathrm{up}}}^{\varepsilon}:=\big[g_{0}(a)+\varepsilon+{\mathrm{i}}a,g_{*}(a)+\varepsilon+{\mathrm{i}}a\big],\;{\widetilde{\Gamma}}_{a,{\mathrm{down}}}^{\varepsilon}=\Big[g_{*}(-a)+\varepsilon-{\mathrm{i}}a,g_{0}(-a)+\varepsilon-{\mathrm{i}}a\Big],

(5.41)

\displaystyle{\widetilde{\Gamma}}_{a,{\mathrm{mid}}}^{\varepsilon}:\lambda=g_{*}(s)+\varepsilon+{\mathrm{i}}s,\;s\in[-a,a].

The path
\Gamma_{a,{\mathrm{mid}}}^{\varepsilon}
was defined in (
3
).

Figure 4. The path
{\widetilde{\Gamma}}_{a}^{\varepsilon}
superimposed over the set
\Omega_{*}
from (
5.3
).

Lemma 5.8
.

Assume Hypotheses (H), (Q), (H-ext),
b:=\inf\limits_{s\in{\mathbb{R}}}g_{0}(s)>-\infty
. Then,

(5.42)

\lim_{a\to\infty}\int_{{\widetilde{\Gamma}}_{a,{\mathrm{up}}}^{\varepsilon}}\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{k}}Q_{i}R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)Q_{j}{\mathrm{d}}\lambda=\lim_{a\to\infty}\int_{{\widetilde{\Gamma}}_{a,{\mathrm{down}}}^{\varepsilon}}\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{k}}Q_{i}R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)Q_{j}{\mathrm{d}}\lambda=0

in the
\mathscr{B}({\mathbb{X}})
-norm, for any
t>\beta_{i,j}
,
\varepsilon\in(0,{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}_{0})
,
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
with
k\geq 2\max\{n_{0},n_{*}\}-1
, where
n_{0}
and
n_{*}
are introduced in (
3.4
) and (
5.5
), respectively. If, in addition,
b_{*}:=\inf_{s\in{\mathbb{R}}}g_{*}(s)>-\infty
, the limits are zero for any
t\geq 0
.

Proof.

The proof of the lemma is similar to the proof of Lemma
3.9
. First, we fix
t\geq 0
,
\varepsilon\in(0,{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}_{0})
and
i,j\in{\mathbb{N}}
. From Hypothesis (H-ext)(i) and since
{\rm Range}(Q_{j})\subseteq{\mathrm{Ker}}(Q_{j+1}-I_{\mathbb{Y}})\subset{\mathbb{X}}_{\infty}
by Lemma
4.2
(ii), we have

(5.43)

R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)Q_{j}=R_{A}(\lambda)_{|{\mathbb{X}}_{\infty}}Q_{j}=R_{A}(\lambda)Q_{j}\;\mbox{for any}\;\lambda\in\Omega_{0}\setminus{\mathscr{M}}.

From (
5
) and (
5.43
) we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{{\widetilde{\Gamma}}_{a,{\mathrm{up}}}^{\varepsilon}}

\displaystyle\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{k}}Q_{i}R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)Q_{j}{\mathrm{d}}\lambda=-\int_{g_{*}(a)+\varepsilon}^{g_{0}(a)+\varepsilon}\frac{e^{(\xi+{\mathrm{i}}a)t}}{(\xi+{\mathrm{i}}a)^{k}}Q_{i}R_{A}^{\infty}(\xi+{\mathrm{i}}a)Q_{j}{\mathrm{d}}\xi

\displaystyle=-\int_{g_{*}(a)+\varepsilon}^{g_{0}(a)}\frac{e^{(\xi+{\mathrm{i}}a)t}}{(\xi+{\mathrm{i}}a)^{k}}Q_{i}R_{A}^{\infty}(\xi+{\mathrm{i}}a)Q_{j}{\mathrm{d}}\xi-\int_{g_{0}(a)}^{g_{0}(a)+\varepsilon}\frac{e^{(\xi+{\mathrm{i}}a)t}}{(\xi+{\mathrm{i}}a)^{k}}Q_{i}R_{A}(\xi+{\mathrm{i}}a)Q_{j}{\mathrm{d}}\xi,

\displaystyle\int_{{\widetilde{\Gamma}}_{a,{\mathrm{down}}}^{\varepsilon}}

\displaystyle\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{k}}Q_{i}R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)Q_{j}{\mathrm{d}}\lambda=\int_{g_{*}(-a)+\varepsilon}^{g_{0}(-a)+\varepsilon}\frac{e^{(\xi-{\mathrm{i}}a)t}}{(\xi-{\mathrm{i}}a)^{k}}Q_{i}R_{A}^{\infty}(\xi-{\mathrm{i}}a)Q_{j}{\mathrm{d}}\xi

(5.44)

\displaystyle=\int_{g_{*}(-a)+\varepsilon}^{g_{0}(-a)}\frac{e^{(\xi-{\mathrm{i}}a)t}}{(\xi-{\mathrm{i}}a)^{k}}Q_{i}R_{A}^{\infty}(\xi-{\mathrm{i}}a)Q_{j}{\mathrm{d}}\xi+\int_{g_{0}(-a)}^{g_{0}(-a)+\varepsilon}\frac{e^{(\xi-{\mathrm{i}}a)t}}{(\xi+{\mathrm{i}}a)^{k}}Q_{i}R_{A}(\xi-{\mathrm{i}}a)Q_{j}{\mathrm{d}}\xi.

From Hypothesis (H-ext)(iii) it follows that

\displaystyle\Bigl\|\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{k}}Q_{i}R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)Q_{j}\Bigr\|

\displaystyle=\frac{e^{({\mathrm{Re}}\lambda)t}}{|\lambda|^{k}}\|Q_{i}R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)Q_{j}\|\leq\frac{e^{({\mathrm{Re}}\lambda)t}}{|\lambda|^{k}}{\widetilde{h}}_{i,j,1}(|\lambda|){\widetilde{h}}_{i,j,2}({\mathrm{Re}}\lambda)

(5.45)

\displaystyle\leq{\widetilde{M}}_{i,j}e^{(t-\beta_{i,j}){\mathrm{Re}}\lambda}{\widetilde{h}}_{i,j,2}(s)\bigr)\frac{{\widetilde{h}}_{i,j,1}(|\lambda|)}{|\lambda|^{k}},\;\mbox{whenever}\;{\mathrm{Re}}\lambda\leq g_{0}({\mathrm{Im}}\lambda),

where
{\widetilde{M}}_{i,j}:=\sup\limits_{s\leq\omega}e^{\beta_{i,j}s}{\widetilde{h}}_{i,j,2}(s)
. We infer that

(5.46)

\Bigg\|\int_{g_{*}(\pm a)+\varepsilon}^{g_{0}(\pm a)}\frac{e^{(\xi\pm{\mathrm{i}}a)t}}{(\xi\pm{\mathrm{i}}a)^{k}}Q_{i}R_{A}^{\infty}(\xi\pm{\mathrm{i}}a)Q_{j}{\mathrm{d}}\xi\Bigg\|\leq{\widetilde{M}}_{i,j}\int_{b_{*}}^{\omega}e^{(t-\beta_{i,j})\xi}\frac{{\widetilde{h}}_{i,j,1}(\sqrt{\xi^{2}+a^{2}})}{(\xi^{2}+a^{2})^{\frac{k}{2}}}{\mathrm{d}}\xi,

where
b_{*}=\inf_{s\in{\mathbb{R}}}g_{*}(s)
. We note that
\sup\limits_{s\in{\mathbb{R}}}g_{0}(s)+{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}_{0}\leq\sup\limits_{s\in{\mathbb{R}}}g_{0}(s)+\varepsilon_{0}<\omega
by (
3.41
). From(
3.46
) we obtain that

(5.47)

\Bigg\|\int_{g_{0}(\pm a)}^{g_{0}(\pm a)+\varepsilon}\frac{e^{(\xi\pm{\mathrm{i}}a)t}}{(\xi\pm{\mathrm{i}}a)^{k}}Q_{i}R_{A}(\xi\pm{\mathrm{i}}a)Q_{j}{\mathrm{d}}\xi\Bigg\|\leq e^{\omega t}\bigl(\sup_{b\leq s\leq\gamma}h_{2}(s)\bigr)\int_{b}^{\omega}\frac{h_{1}(\sqrt{\xi^{2}+a^{2}})}{(\xi^{2}+a^{2})^{\frac{k}{2}}}{\mathrm{d}}\xi.

for any
t\geq 0
. One can readily check that

(5.48)

e^{(t-\beta_{i,j}\cdot)}\chi_{(b_{*},\omega]}\in L^{1}({\mathbb{R}})\;\mbox{whenever}\;t>\beta_{i,j}\;\mbox{or}\;t\geq 0,b_{*}>-\infty.

Since
\lim\limits_{s\to\infty}\frac{{\widetilde{h}}_{i,j,1}(s)}{s^{k}}=0
, from Remark
3.8
and (
5.48
) we conclude that

(5.49)

\lim_{a\to\infty}\int_{b_{*}}^{\omega}e^{(t-\beta_{i,j})\xi}\frac{{\widetilde{h}}_{i,j,1}(\sqrt{\xi^{2}+a^{2}})}{(\xi^{2}+a^{2})^{\frac{k}{2}}}{\mathrm{d}}\xi=0\;\mbox{whenever}\;t>\beta_{i,j}\;\mbox{or}\;t\geq 0,b_{*}>-\infty.

The lemma follows from (
3.48
), (
5
), (
5.46
), (
5.47
), (
5.49
).
∎

Remark 5.9
.

From (
5.40
) and Hypotheses (H) and (H-ext) one can readily check that the interior of the set enclosed by the path
{\widetilde{\Gamma}}_{a}^{\varepsilon}
contains all the resonances
\nu_{\ell}
,
\ell=1,\dots,p
, and does not contain any of the singularities
\mu_{k}
,
k=1,\dots,m
, for any
a>\max_{1\leq\ell\leq p}|{\mathrm{Im}}\nu_{\ell}|
,
\varepsilon\in(0,{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}_{0})
. Moreover,
0\notin\mathrm{Range}({\widetilde{\Gamma}}_{a,{\mathrm{mid}}}^{\varepsilon})
. In addition,
0
belongs to the interior of the set enclosed by
{\widetilde{\Gamma}}_{a}^{\varepsilon}
if and only if
g_{*}(0)<0\leq g_{0}(0)
.

Let
P_{k}
and
N_{k}\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
k=1,\dots,m
be defined in Lemma
3.3
,
{\widetilde{P}}_{\ell}
,
{\widehat{P}}_{\ell}
,
{\widetilde{N}}_{\ell}
,
{\widehat{N}}_{\ell}
,
\ell=1,\dots,p
, be defined in Lemma

6.
Frechet Space estimates of integral terms

In this subsection we aim to estimate the integral terms in the representations of cosine and sine functions from Theorem
5.10
and Theorem
5.11
. There are some similarities to the estimates of similar integral terms in Section
3
. However there are important differences. We have seen that the curve
{\mathrm{Re}}\lambda=g_{0}({\mathrm{Im}}\lambda)
needs to be to the right of the set
\{\lambda\in{\mathbb{C}}:\lambda^{2}\in\sigma_{\mathrm{ess}}(A)\}
, therefore it is natural to assume that
\inf_{s\in{\mathbb{R}}}g_{0}(s)>-\infty
. The curve
{\mathrm{Re}}\lambda=g_{*}({\mathrm{Im}}\lambda)
does not need to satisfy such conditions and in concrete examples (cosine families generated by a Schrödinger operator) it might be possible to choose a function
g_{*}
such that
g_{*}(s)=\mathscr{O}(-\ln{|s|})
as
s\to\pm\infty
.

Another important difference is that the extension
R_{A}^{\infty}
does not, in general, satisfy the Hilbert resolvent identity. More precisely, one can readily check that, in general, the range of
R(\mu^{2},A)
is not necessarily contained in
{\mathbb{X}}_{\infty}
. Indeed, if
A=\partial_{x}^{2}
one can readily check that
{\mathbb{X}}_{\infty}=L^{2}_{\mathrm{comp}}({\mathbb{R}})
, however
R(1,\partial_{x}^{2})\chi_{[0,1]}\notin L^{2}_{\mathrm{comp}}({\mathbb{R}})
. This shows that, in general,
R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)
cannot be applied to elements of the range of
R(\mu^{2},A)
, and thus
R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)R(\mu^{2},A)
needed for the Hilbert resolvent identity does not make sense.

First, we estimate the integral terms of the representations given in Theorem
5.10
and Theorem
5.11
, when the path
{\mathrm{Re}}\lambda=g_{*}({\mathrm{Im}}\lambda)+\varepsilon
is replaced by the path
{\widetilde{\Gamma}}_{a,{\mathrm{mid}}}^{\varepsilon}
, see Figure 4.

Lemma 6.1
.

Assume Hypotheses (H), (Q) and (H-ext). Then, the following assertions hold true:

(i)

There exists
{\widetilde{M}}_{\mathrm{mid}}^{\mathrm{c}}:{\mathbb{N}}^{3}\times(0,\infty)\times(0,\varepsilon_{0})\to(0,\infty)
such that

(6.1)

\Big\|\int_{{\widetilde{\Gamma}}_{a,{\mathrm{mid}}}^{\varepsilon}}\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n-1}}Q_{i}R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)A^{n}x{\mathrm{d}}\lambda\Big\|\leq M_{\mathrm{mid}}^{\mathrm{c}}(n,i,j,a,\varepsilon)e^{(\sup_{s\in{\mathbb{R}}}g_{*}(s)+\varepsilon)t}\|A^{n-1}x\|

for any
t\geq 0
,
x\in\text{\rm{dom}}(A^{n})\cap{\mathrm{Ker}}(Q_{j}-I_{\mathbb{Y}})
,
i,j\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
\varepsilon\in(0,{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}_{0})
,
a>0
;

(ii)

There exists
{\widetilde{M}}_{\mathrm{mid}}^{\mathrm{s}}:{\mathbb{N}}^{3}\times(0,\infty)\times(0,{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}_{0})\to(0,\infty)
such that

(6.2)

\Big\|Q_{i}A\int_{{\widetilde{\Gamma}}_{a,{\mathrm{mid}}}^{\varepsilon}}\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n}}Q_{i+1}R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)A^{n-1}w{\mathrm{d}}\lambda\Big\|\leq{\widetilde{M}}_{\mathrm{mid}}^{\mathrm{s}}(n,i,j,a,\varepsilon)e^{(\sup_{s\in{\mathbb{R}}}g_{*}(s)+\varepsilon)t}\|A^{n-1}w\|

for any
t\geq 0
,
x\in{\mathbb{W}}_{n-1}\cap{\mathrm{Ker}}(Q_{j}-I_{\mathbb{Y}})
,
i,j\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
\varepsilon\in(0,{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}_{0})
,
a>0
.

Proof.

Fix
t\geq 0
,
i,j\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
x\in\text{\rm{dom}}(A^{n})\cap{\mathrm{Ker}}(Q_{j}-I_{\mathbb{Y}})
,
w\in{\mathbb{W}}_{n-1}\cap{\mathrm{Ker}}(Q_{j}-I_{\mathbb{Y}})
,
\varepsilon\in(0,{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}_{0})
. From Lemma
5.2
(iii) we infer that
A^{n}x,A^{n-1}w\in{\mathrm{Ker}}(Q_{j}-I_{\mathbb{Y}})
, hence
A^{n}x=Q_{j}A^{n}x
and
A^{n-1}w=Q_{j}A^{n-1}w
. From Lemma
5.2
(i) it follows that

(6.3)

\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n-1}}Q_{i}R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)A^{n}x=\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n-3}}Q_{i}R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)Q_{j}A^{n-1}x-\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n-1}}Q_{i}A^{n-1}x

(6.4)

\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n}}Q_{i}{\widetilde{A}}R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)Q_{j}A^{n-1}w=\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n-2}}Q_{i}R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)Q_{j}A^{n-1}w-\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n}}Q_{i}A^{n-1}w

for any
\lambda\in\Omega_{*}\setminus\bigl({\mathscr{M}}\cup{\mathscr{N}}\cup\{0\}\bigr)
. Assertion (i) follows from (
6.3
) for

\displaystyle{\widetilde{M}}_{\mathrm{mid}}^{\mathrm{c}}(n,i,j,a,\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=\Bigl(\max\{{\widetilde{M}}_{1,{\mathrm{mid}}}^{\mathrm{c}}(n,a,\varepsilon),M_{2,{\mathrm{mid}}}^{\mathrm{c}}(n,i,a,\varepsilon)\}\Bigr)\mathrm{Lenght}({\widetilde{\Gamma}}_{a,{\mathrm{mid}}}^{\varepsilon}),\;\mbox{where}

(6.5)

\displaystyle{\widetilde{M}}_{1,{\mathrm{mid}}}^{\mathrm{c}}(n,i,j,a,\varepsilon)

\displaystyle:=\sup_{\lambda\in\mathrm{Range}({\widetilde{\Gamma}}_{a,{\mathrm{mid}}}^{\varepsilon})}\frac{\|Q_{i}R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)Q_{j}\|}{|\lambda|^{2n-3}},\;{\widetilde{M}}_{2,{\mathrm{mid}}}^{\mathrm{c}}(n,i,a,\varepsilon):=\sup_{\lambda\in\mathrm{Range}({\widetilde{\Gamma}}_{a,{\mathrm{mid}}}^{\varepsilon})}\frac{q_{*}}{|\lambda|^{2n-1}},

where
q_{*}
is defined in (
4.7
). Since the linear operator
A
is closed and
Q_{i}\in{\mathscr{C}}{\mathscr{L}}({\mathbb{Y}},{\mathbb{X}})
, from
[
2
, Proposition 1.1.7]
and (
Q_{7}
) we infer that

(6.6)

Q_{i}A\int_{{\widetilde{\Gamma}}_{a,{\mathrm{mid}}}^{\varepsilon}}\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n}}Q_{i+1}R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)A^{n-1}w{\mathrm{d}}\lambda=\int_{{\widetilde{\Gamma}}_{a,{\mathrm{mid}}}^{\varepsilon}}\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n}}Q_{i}{\widetilde{A}}R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)A^{n-1}w{\mathrm{d}}\lambda.

Assertion (ii) follows from (
6.4
) and (
6.6
) for

\displaystyle{\widetilde{M}}_{\mathrm{mid}}^{\mathrm{s}}(n,i,j,a,\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=\Bigl(\max\{{\widetilde{M}}_{1,{\mathrm{mid}}}^{\mathrm{s}}(n,i,j,a,\varepsilon),M_{2,{\mathrm{mid}}}^{\mathrm{s}}(n,i,a,\varepsilon)\}\Bigr)\mathrm{Lenght}({\widetilde{\Gamma}}_{a,{\mathrm{mid}}}^{\varepsilon}),\;\mbox{where}

(6.7)

\displaystyle{\widetilde{M}}_{1,{\mathrm{mid}}}^{\mathrm{s}}(n,i,j,a,\varepsilon)

\displaystyle:=\sup_{\lambda\in\mathrm{Range}({\widetilde{\Gamma}}_{a,{\mathrm{mid}}}^{\varepsilon})}\frac{\|Q_{i}R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)Q_{j}\|}{|\lambda|^{2n-2}},\;M_{2,{\mathrm{mid}}}^{\mathrm{s}}(n,i,a,\varepsilon):=\sup_{\lambda\in\mathrm{Range}({\widetilde{\Gamma}}_{a,{\mathrm{mid}}}^{\varepsilon})}\frac{q_{*}}{|\lambda|^{2n}}.

∎

Similar to Section
3
we define the path

(6.8)

{\widetilde{\Gamma}}_{a,{\mathrm{out}}}^{\varepsilon}:\lambda=g_{*}(s)+\varepsilon+{\mathrm{i}}s,\;s\in(-\infty,-a]\cup[a,\infty).

Our next task is to prove estimates of the integral terms in the representations given in Theorem
5.10
and Theorem
5.11
, when the path
{\mathrm{Re}}\lambda=g_{*}({\mathrm{Im}}\lambda)+\varepsilon
is replaced by the path
{\widetilde{\Gamma}}_{a,{\mathrm{out}}}^{\varepsilon}
. To prove these results we introduce

(6.9)

{\overline{a}}_{*}=\max\{\max_{1\leq k\leq m}|\mu_{k}|,\max_{1\leq\ell\leq p}|\nu_{\ell}|\}+1.

One important assumption on the function
g_{*}
, introduced in Hypothesis (H-ext) is

Hypothesis (G).
We assume that there exist
c_{*}>0
,
{\overline{s}}_{*}>{\overline{a}}_{*}
and
{\varkappa}_{*}\geq 0
, such that

(i)

g_{*}(s)\leq-c_{*}\ln{|s|}
whenever
|s|\geq{\overline{s}}_{*}
;

(ii)

e^{{\varkappa}_{*}g_{*}}g_{*}^{\prime}\in L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}})
.

First, we will prove two preliminary estimates needed in the sequel.

Lemma 6.2
.

Assume Hypotheses (H), (Q), (H-ext) and (G) and, in addition,
b:=\inf\limits_{s\in{\mathbb{R}}}g_{0}(s)>-\infty
. Then,

(i)

There exists
\,\mbox{\bf M}_{{\mathrm{out}},1}:{\mathbb{N}}_{2n_{*}-1}\times{\mathbb{N}}^{2}\to(0,\infty)
such that

(6.10)

\Big\|\int_{{\widetilde{\Gamma}}_{a,{\mathrm{out}}}^{\varepsilon}}\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{k-2}}Q_{i}R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)Q_{j}{\mathrm{d}}\lambda\Big\|\leq\frac{\,\mbox{\bf M}_{{\mathrm{out}},1}(k,i,j)e^{\varepsilon(t-\beta_{i,j})}}{\big(c_{*}(t-\beta_{i,j}-{\varkappa}_{*})-3\big)a^{c_{*}(t-\beta_{i,j}-{\varkappa}_{*})-3}}

for any
t>\beta_{i,j}+{\varkappa}_{*}+3/c_{*}
,
i,j\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
a\geq{\overline{s}}_{*}
,
\varepsilon\in(0,{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}_{0})
,
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
with
k\geq 2n_{*}-1
;

(ii)

There exists
\,\mbox{\bf M}_{{\mathrm{out}},2}>0
such that

(6.11)

\Big|\int_{{\widetilde{\Gamma}}_{a,{\mathrm{out}}}^{\varepsilon}}\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{k}}{\mathrm{d}}\lambda\Big|\leq\frac{\,\mbox{\bf M}_{{\mathrm{out}},2}e^{\varepsilon t}}{\big(c_{*}(t-{\varkappa}_{*})+k-1\big)a^{c_{*}(t-{\varkappa}_{*})+k-1}}

for any
t>{\varkappa}_{*}-(k-1)/c_{*}
,
i,j\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
a\geq{\overline{s}}_{*}
,
\varepsilon\in(0,{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}_{0})
.

Proof.

Fix
t\geq 0
,
k,i,j\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
a\geq{\overline{s}}_{*}
,
\varepsilon\in(0,{\widetilde{\varepsilon}})
. First, we note that by our assumptions on
{\widetilde{h}}_{i,j,1}
and
{\widetilde{h}}_{i,j,2}
,

\displaystyle{\overline{M}}_{*}(k,i,j)

\displaystyle:=\Bigg(\max\Big\{\sup_{s\geq 1}\frac{{\widetilde{h}}_{i,j,1}(s)}{s^{k}},\,\sup_{s\leq\omega}e^{\beta_{i,j}s}{\widetilde{h}}_{i,j,2}(s)\Big\}\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}<\infty

(6.12)

\displaystyle M_{*,1}

\displaystyle:=\mathrm{esssup}_{s\in{\mathbb{R}}}|e^{{\varkappa}_{*}g_{*}(s)}g_{*}^{\prime}(s)|<\infty.

From Hypotheses (H) and (H-ext) we know that

(6.13)

g_{*}(s)+\varepsilon<g_{*}(s)+{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}\leq g_{*}(s)+\delta_{0}\leq g_{0}(s)\leq\omega\;\mbox{for any}\;s\in{\mathbb{R}}.

Hence, from (
6
) and (
6.13
) we obtain the following estimate

\displaystyle\Big\|\int_{{\widetilde{\Gamma}}_{a,{\mathrm{out}}}^{\varepsilon}}\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{k-2}}Q_{i}R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)Q_{j}{\mathrm{d}}\lambda\Big\|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{|s|\geq a}\frac{e^{(g_{*}(s)+\varepsilon)t}}{|g_{*}(s)+\varepsilon+{\mathrm{i}}s|^{k-2}}{\widetilde{h}}_{i,j,1}(|g_{*}(s)+\varepsilon+{\mathrm{i}}s|){\widetilde{h}}_{i,j,2}(g_{*}(s)+\varepsilon)|g_{*}^{\prime}(s)+{\mathrm{i}}|{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle\leq{\overline{M}}_{*}(k,i,j)e^{{\varkappa}_{*}\varepsilon}\int_{|s|\geq a}|g_{*}(s)+\varepsilon+{\mathrm{i}}s|^{2}e^{(t-\beta_{i,j}-{\varkappa}_{*})(g_{*}(s)+\varepsilon)}\big|e^{{\varkappa}_{*}g_{*}(s)}g_{*}^{\prime}(s)+{\mathrm{i}}e^{{\varkappa}_{*}g_{*}(s)}\big|{\mathrm{d}}s

(6.14)

\displaystyle\leq{\overline{M}}_{*}(k,i,j)(M_{*,1}+e^{{\varkappa}_{*}\omega})e^{{\varkappa}_{*}\varepsilon}\int_{|s|\geq a}|g_{*}(s)+\varepsilon+{\mathrm{i}}s|^{2}e^{(t-\beta_{i,j}-{\varkappa}_{*})(g_{*}(s)+\varepsilon)}{\mathrm{d}}s.

Next, we estimate

\displaystyle\int_{|s|\geq a}

\displaystyle s^{2}e^{(t-\beta_{i,j}-{\varkappa}_{*})(g_{*}(s)+\varepsilon)}{\mathrm{d}}s\leq e^{\varepsilon(t-\beta_{i,j}-{\varkappa}_{*})}\int_{|s|\geq a}s^{2}e^{-c_{*}(t-\beta_{i,j}-{\varkappa}_{*})\ln{|s|}}{\mathrm{d}}s

(6.15)

\displaystyle=2e^{\varepsilon(t-\beta_{i,j}-{\varkappa}_{*})}\int_{a}^{\infty}\frac{{\mathrm{d}}s}{s^{c_{*}(t-\beta_{i,j}-{\varkappa}_{*})-2}}=\frac{2e^{\varepsilon(t-\beta_{i,j}-{\varkappa}_{*})}}{\big(c_{*}(t-\beta_{i,j}-{\varkappa}_{*})-3\big)a^{c_{*}(t-\beta_{i,j}-{\varkappa}_{*})-3}}.

for any
t>\beta_{i,j}+{\varkappa}_{*}+3/c_{*}
. Next, we define
M_{*,2}=\sup_{\xi\leq\omega}\xi^{2}e^{\frac{\xi}{c_{*}}}<\infty
. From (
6.13
) it follows that

\displaystyle\int_{|s|\geq a}(g_{*}(s)+\varepsilon)^{2}e^{(t-\beta_{i,j}-{\varkappa}_{*})(g_{*}(s)+\varepsilon)}{\mathrm{d}}s\leq\int_{|s|\geq a}(g_{*}(s)+\varepsilon)^{2}e^{\frac{g_{*}(s)+\varepsilon}{c_{*}}}e^{(t-\beta_{i,j}-{\varkappa}_{*}-1/c_{*})(g_{*}(s)+\varepsilon)}{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle\leq M_{*,2}\int_{|s|\geq a}e^{(t-\beta_{i,j}-{\varkappa}_{*}-1/c_{*})(g_{*}(s)+\varepsilon)}{\mathrm{d}}s\leq M_{*,2}e^{\varepsilon(t-\beta_{i,j}-{\varkappa}_{*}-1/c_{*})}\int_{|s|\geq a}e^{-c_{*}(t-\beta_{i,j}-{\varkappa}_{*}-1/c_{*})\ln{|s|}}{\mathrm{d}}s

(6.16)

\displaystyle\leq 2M_{*,2}e^{\varepsilon(t-\beta_{i,j}-{\varkappa}_{*})}\int_{a}^{\infty}\frac{{\mathrm{d}}s}{s^{c_{*}(t-\beta_{i,j}-{\varkappa}_{*})-1}}=\frac{2M_{*,2}e^{\varepsilon(t-\beta_{i,j}-{\varkappa}_{*})}}{\big(c_{*}(t-\beta_{i,j}-{\varkappa}_{*})-2\big)a^{c_{*}(t-\beta_{i,j}-{\varkappa}_{*})-2}}.

for any
t>\beta_{i,j}+{\varkappa}_{*}+2/c_{*}
. From (
6
), (
6
) and (
6
) we conclude that

(6.17)

\Big\|\int_{{\widetilde{\Gamma}}_{a,{\mathrm{out}}}^{\varepsilon}}\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{k-2}}Q_{i}R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)Q_{j}{\mathrm{d}}\lambda\Big\|\leq\,\mbox{\bf M}_{{\mathrm{out}},1}(k,i,j)\frac{e^{\varepsilon(t-\beta_{i,j})}}{\big(c_{*}(t-\beta_{i,j}-{\varkappa}_{*})-3\big)a^{c_{*}(t-\beta_{i,j}-{\varkappa}_{*})-3}}.

for any
t>\beta_{i,j}+{\varkappa}_{*}+3/c_{*}
, where
\,\mbox{\bf M}_{{\mathrm{out}},1}(k,i,j):=2{\overline{M}}_{*}(k,i,j)(M_{*,1}+e^{{\varkappa}_{*}\omega})\max\{1,M_{*,2}/{\overline{s}}_{*}\}
, proving assertion (i). Similarly,

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{{\widetilde{\Gamma}}_{a,{\mathrm{out}}}^{\varepsilon}}

\displaystyle\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{k}}{\mathrm{d}}\lambda\Big|\leq\int_{|s|\geq a}\frac{e^{(g_{*}(s)+\varepsilon)t}|g_{*}^{\prime}(s)+{\mathrm{i}}|}{|g_{*}(s)+\varepsilon+{\mathrm{i}}s|^{k}}{\mathrm{d}}s\leq e^{\varepsilon t}\int_{|s|\geq a}\frac{e^{(t-{\varkappa}_{*})g_{*}(s)}}{|s|^{k}}\big|e^{{\varkappa}_{*}g_{*}(s)}g_{*}^{\prime}(s)+{\mathrm{i}}e^{{\varkappa}_{*}g_{*}(s)}\big|{\mathrm{d}}s

(6.18)

\displaystyle\leq(M_{*,1}+e^{{\varkappa}_{*}\omega})e^{\varepsilon t}\int_{|s|\geq a}\frac{e^{-c_{*}(t-{\varkappa}_{*})\ln{|s|}}}{|s|^{k}}{\mathrm{d}}s=(M_{*,1}+e^{{\varkappa}_{*}\omega})e^{\varepsilon t}\int_{|s|\geq a}\frac{{\mathrm{d}}s}{|s|^{c_{*}(t-{\varkappa}_{*})+k}}.

for any
t>{\varkappa}_{*}-(k-1)/c_{*}
. Assertion (ii) follows shortly from (
6
). ∎

Lemma 6.3
.

Assume Hypotheses (H), (Q), (H-ext), (G) and, in addition,
b:=\inf\limits_{s\in{\mathbb{R}}}g_{0}(s)>-\infty
. Then, there exists
{\widetilde{M}}^{\mathrm{c}}_{\mathrm{out}}:{\mathbb{N}}_{\max\{n_{0},n_{*}\}}\times{\mathbb{N}}^{2}\to(0,\infty)
such that

(6.19)

\Big\|\int_{{\widetilde{\Gamma}}_{a,{\mathrm{out}}}^{\varepsilon}}\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n-1}}Q_{i}R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)A^{n}x{\mathrm{d}}\lambda\Big\|\leq\frac{{\widetilde{M}}^{\mathrm{c}}_{\mathrm{out}}(n,i,j)e^{\varepsilon(t-\beta_{i,j})}\|A^{n-1}x\|}{\big(c_{*}(t-\beta_{i,j}-{\varkappa}_{*})-3\big)a^{c_{*}(t-\beta_{i,j}-{\varkappa}_{*})-3}}

for any
t>\beta_{i,j}+{\varkappa}_{*}+3/c_{*}
,
i,j\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
a\geq{\overline{s}}_{*}
,
x\in\text{\rm{dom}}(A^{n})\cap{\mathrm{Ker}}(Q_{j}-I_{\mathbb{Y}})
,
\varepsilon\in(0,{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}_{0})
,
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
, with
n\geq\max\{n_{0},n_{*}\}
.

Proof.

Fix
t\geq 0
,
i,j\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
a\geq{\overline{s}}_{*}
,

,
\varepsilon\in(0,{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}_{0})
.
From (
6.3
) it follows that

\int_{{\widetilde{\Gamma}}_{a,{\mathrm{out}}}^{\varepsilon}}\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n-1}}Q_{i}R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)A^{n}x{\mathrm{d}}\lambda=\int_{{\widetilde{\Gamma}}_{a,{\mathrm{out}}}^{\varepsilon}}\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n-3}}Q_{i}R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)Q_{j}A^{n-1}x{\mathrm{d}}\lambda-\int_{{\widetilde{\Gamma}}_{a,{\mathrm{out}}}^{\varepsilon}}\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n-1}}{\mathrm{d}}\lambda Q_{i}A^{n-1}x.

We define
{\widetilde{M}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{\mathrm{c}}:{\mathbb{N}}_{\max\{n_{0},n_{*}\}}\times{\mathbb{N}}^{2}\to(0,\infty)
by

(6.21)

{\widetilde{M}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{\mathrm{c}}(n,i,j)=\,\mbox{\bf M}_{{\mathrm{out}},1}(2n-1,i,j)+q_{*}\,\mbox{\bf M}_{{\mathrm{out}},2}e^{{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}_{0}\beta_{i,j}},

4.7
). The lemma follows shortly from Lemma

(with

), (
6.20
) and (

).
∎

for any
t>\beta_{i,j}+{\varkappa}_{*}+3/c_{*}
,
i,j\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
a\geq{\overline{s}}_{*}
,

\varepsilon\in(0,{\widetilde{\varepsilon}}_{0})
,

, with
n\geq\max\{n_{0},n_{*}\}

Proof.

Fix
t\geq 0
,
i,j\in{\mathbb{N}}
,

,

\displaystyle Q_{i}A

\displaystyle\int_{{\widetilde{\Gamma}}_{a,{\mathrm{out}}}^{\varepsilon}}\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n}}Q_{i+1}R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)A^{n-1}w{\mathrm{d}}\lambda=\int_{{\widetilde{\Gamma}}_{a,{\mathrm{out}}}^{\varepsilon}}\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n}}Q_{i}{\widetilde{A}}R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)A^{n-1}w{\mathrm{d}}\lambda

(6.23)

\displaystyle=\int_{{\widetilde{\Gamma}}_{a,{\mathrm{out}}}^{\varepsilon}}\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n-2}}Q_{i}R_{A}^{\infty}(\lambda)Q_{j}A^{n-1}w{\mathrm{d}}\lambda-\int_{{\widetilde{\Gamma}}_{a,{\mathrm{out}}}^{\varepsilon}}\frac{e^{\lambda t}}{\lambda^{2n}}{\mathrm{d}}\lambda Q_{i}A^{n-1}w.

{\widetilde{M}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{\mathrm{c}}:{\mathbb{N}}_{\max\{n_{0},n_{*}\}}\times{\mathbb{N}}^{2}\to(0,\infty)
